ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23400	Thank you, well-respected President of the Croatian Parliament, respected MPs and MPs, the Law on textbooks for elementary and high school was adopted in 2006, and the Law on Supplements to the Law on elementary and secondary school textbooks was adopted at the end of that year, which was then determined to provide textbooks and other educational means for compulsory education with the resources of the state budget. Thus, the availability of education is facilitated, especially by the most significant educational population, or children. I will also remind you that in June 2007 a national programme of measures for the introduction of compulsory secondary education was adopted, and in this document that you have adopted, so-called. encouraging measures because we did not want to impose compulsory education by legal coercion, but gradually by encouraging measures, so when the cycle is over then we can also think about norming. Among other incentive measures, it has been said that the textbooks and resources of the state budget for first-class students will be provided, 2007/2008. and then gradually year by year and for other generations of students until the cycle is complete. The application of the law at this time has so far pointed to certain difficulties and not consistency, above all in the determination, selection and cost of textbooks and, ultimately, to the distribution of textbooks. In accordance with the applicable law, the textbook implies an unlimited number of supplementary teaching tools, so work notebooks, collection of tasks, geographical and historical atlases, which are printed separately from the textbook as a basic teaching tool. As a result of that provision, the textbooks have increased the number of different supplementary teaching tools that are not necessarily necessary for the implementation of the teaching process. The draft law amending and supplementing the Law on elementary and secondary school textbooks with the final draft law separates the work notebook, the collection of tasks, the geographical atlas, the historical atlas, the art map and the electronic textbook as other teaching means from the concept of textbooks instead of the current definition of the supplementary teaching tool as a textbook. This significantly facilitates the planning and acquisition of textbooks. So, so far, we have been in a position to pay from the state budget funds what the school ordered, and many times this with the textbook was three or four additional teaching tools, which of course not only increased the price, but also increased the number of textbooks that the student should wear to school. The draft law sets out how the state budget provides funds for the procurement of textbooks and other educational means in primary and secondary schools according to the number of students and limits for the purchase of textbooks and other curricula by a student who is determined depending on the type of school, education programme and class, starting with the average cost, the production of textbooks and these limits is prescribed by the Ministry. On the basis of their needs and secured resources according to the limits, a student, or a teacher, chooses textbooks and other educational means and the school implements their public procurement process, unlike the way they have been distributed free textbooks conducted by the Education and Education Agency and in which schools have no financial limitations in ordering textbooks. We predict that not using teaching tools, schools can be used to obtain practical methodical means and aids and rules on the preservation and use of textbooks and other educational means of school are prescribed by their general instruments. Schools are required after the publication of the textbook catalogue and no later than 31 March start the procurement procedure. For the purchase of textbooks in these amendments and additions to the laws, we also provide for the establishment of monetary participations, parents, or guardians, students and the manner of payment of monetary participation based on a personal identification number will be prescribed by the minister. The procedure for the approval of new textbooks for the next school year would begin on new dates, so it is always necessary to set one date per year, namely, the tender has been continuous, it has been opened all year, but it is necessary to set the date due to procedures in the ministry itself. So far, it's December 1st, we're now predicting it to be September 1st. With the request to approve the textbooks, the publishers were to deliver six copies, we now predict that it should be less, that it should be 4. Furthermore, in the process of approving textbooks, commissions participate, so the author is undoubtedly an expert on a particular subject, it is the first level, in the second level of two reviewers, giving their reviews on the proposed manuscript, and then in the ministry, the committee assesses according to the textbook's curricula and programme and in some situations determines the need for textbooks, it is a protection for those extreme situations so that it would not happen that some textbook comes into the first class, and even the work book and children have not yet learned to read and write, so here I underline only in such extreme situations the expert commission will assess whether it is necessary, of course, in the largest number of cases not. So far, the professional commissions have had five members, and now we propose to be three members, so we don't really disrupt the structure, we'll remain an expert in the area, we'll remain a Methodist and remain a practitioner. So far, there have been three practitioners, we believe that this is not necessary, and that will actually reduce the cost of commission work. I'll remind you here that we hire about 550 experts every year. Now that number will be significantly smaller, I would say, not only will the funds be saved but it will actually be able to be faster, or more expedient, because while all five are studying the texts, while they agree, it will take a little longer, and this time we think that the time will still be summed up. By proposing the law, this law makes it impossible to practice the multiple appearance of the same publishers, and we have here predicted that one publisher is running for one textbook for one subject. So by now it could have been more. We further restrict the possibility of frequent textbook changes in this proposal. So far, it has been up to 20% that it can be changed, but now we say if these are substantive changes because of what has been known in some cases to happen to change some photos, change some charts, some illustrations, and then there's this famous percentage. We still think it's better to attach to that content part. So we're giving up the new textbooks as we've determined 3+3. We agreed here that this is necessary for quality and that these three that are in reserve will actually contribute to the quality of the textbook and bind to a percentage, so up to 10%. We think that's a solution that was good. We have prevented a conflict of interest related to family ties with publishers or authors, and now we have expanded it so that they cannot be in either, so members of the expert commission can no longer be in a business relationship which is actually very important and we feel that this should certainly be introduced. And when it comes to the withdrawal of textbooks so far, it has been a solution to do so together by the publishers, but since the author and the publisher have a mutual contract, it is quite enough to say that it is the publisher, so we have now decided on such a solution. And at the end of the respected MPs and MPs, we believe that this is how we introduce restrictions per student, according to our educational programme, that we will be able to plan better. There's always a plan size. We now have this plan size in the state budget and we hope to make it much more rational this year. Namely, the solution that was left for us would probably take about 650 million kunas to get new textbooks, only to enter the third grade of high school, and this solution provides that the entire system and the third and fourth classes should be included so that all students are in the same position. I am ultimately obliged to say that our parent committee, of course, and the other committees, have discussed this bill very thoroughly. I thank you, I thank you for this help, Honorable President Selem, and we are inclined to accept these amendments which you will present in your address to the Croatian Assembly, so that your argument that it is not one textbook from one publisher is indeed in place and you have made a good judgment because it could indeed happen if a publisher is running two textbooks. One gets 30%, the other gets 20% to lose the 20%, and that's not good. As well as you have assessed, of course, when it comes to obtaining more centrally and that it can be more favourable than any school that has never conducted this procurement procedure. And ultimately, your suggestion when it comes to "curiculum" is really acceptable, so we will gladly accept such solutions by keeping one piece of it in transitional provisions until we do, then we will apply curricula and programmes. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23402	Mr. President, Mr. Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues. This is one of the most important items in the Committee on Education, Science and Culture lately. The debate was very thorough. The Committee considered the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on elementary and secondary textbooks at its 10th session on 4 December of this year. And that debated him clearly as a parent working body. The opening argument was tabled by the applicant's representative. Thus, the proposal certainly brings about an amendment to the Law on elementary and secondary textbooks published in "National Journals" No. 36 since March 2006 and has been supplemented by the Law on Supplements to the Law on textbooks for elementary and secondary schools since December 2006. The introductory text of this proposal shows that the proposed Government of the Republic of Croatia points out 12 reasons why it proposes amendments and additions. These include: improvement in the system and work of expert committees to assess the need to use textbooks and determine compliance with the curriculum. Furthermore, the change in the definition of textbooks and supplementary teaching tools. Then the ratio of the state budget to the acquisition of textbooks that would basically remain free but establish restrictions, participation. Then preventing the possibility of one publisher offering more textbooks for one teaching course in the same period and spreading the concept of conflict of interest to cases where members of expert committees are not only in the family who has been so far but in a business relationship with publishers and authors of textbooks. The discussion points out the disagreement between the definition of textbooks in Article 1 and Article 2 of the Law, since Article 1 of the textbook considers itself to be a basic teaching tool in the form of a book while Article 2 provides that the textbook is a basic teaching tool published in printed and other forms. The participants of the debate also paid attention to the proposal on the participation of pupils or parents on the occasion of obtaining textbooks by raising the issue of budgetary and financial restrictions, by means of objectively establishing a social moment if participation was introduced and, in particular, with organisational issues, as well as the role of a sublegal act that would bring the issue closer to regulation. Among the issues for the debate, it was found that the proposal was not the best solution... the proposed restriction referred to in Article 18, according to which only one textbook with the highest percentage of representation for each teaching course in one class of the same education programme could be introduced. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] The second issue raised by the biggest debate was the regime for the procurement of textbooks under the conditions of legally regulated public procurement of state authorities and users of the state budget. Participants agree that the proposed solution should be avoided, which would lead to more than 1300 schools conducting tenders or a prescribed way of public procurement and a solution aimed at uniting the tendering and implementation of tenders preferably at the Education and Education Agency. A critical review shall be submitted to the debate and a solution whereby, leading its own policy, the procurement of school textbooks could be a means that would thus possibly save the use of the funds for the purpose provided for in Article 4(5) of the Law, in particular given the possibility of a permanent abuse of such a solution that the applicant certainly did not take account of. The above issues have found the largest part of the terms in the amendments to the draft legislation proposed by the Committee. In addition, some fundamental issues have been highlighted in the debate, the underlying reasons for the alleged populational decline in the quality of textbooks. It is also indicated that other circumstances could raise the quality of textbooks, the composition of evaluation bodies, the anonymity of applications and the like. After the debate, the committee concluded, by majority, votes 7 for and 2 abstentions. It is proposed to the Croatian Parliament to pass the Law on textbooks for primary and secondary schools along with the following amendments. I don't think there's any need for Mr. President to read those amendments because they're attached to the text. I would just say that a very nice co-operation has been achieved in the formulation of these amendments between the committee members and the proposing representatives. So, indeed, better solutions have been sought, and I am convinced that these amendments that are before you really contribute to the law that we are going to adopt today or these days be better and better quality, and that it clearly corresponds to what is most important to us, which are our children and our disciples. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23404	Thank you, Mr. President. The Committee on Human Rights and the Rights of National Minorities has debated this law thoroughly because it is intruding into some fundamental human rights and minority rights in some parts. A number of comments were raised in the debate on the proposed solutions, with an assessment that some of them could, in practice, be able to address solutions that might in practice lead to additional difficulties, and certain provisions and different interpretations which question the very purpose of the proposed amendments and amendments to the law. When it comes to the introduction of participation of parents, i.e. custodian for the purchase of textbooks and other curricula, the opinion has been expressed that it is a matter concerning the rights of citizens, so it is not acceptable for the minister to decide on this without any legal criteria laid down as provided for in Article 15. Final bill. It has been warned that the rights and criteria for their implementation can only be prescribed by law. It is also pointed out that such a proposal encroachs on children's rights, creating differences between them based on the property of their parents, and this is not about the rights of parents but on the rights of children. A number of remarks have also been made to the provisions relating to textbooks and other teaching tools in the language and letter of national minorities. It has been pointed out that this teaching has been followed for many years by the problem of the lack of necessary textbooks, and that the proposed solutions in the final bill will not only help solve this problem, but that some of them can further complicate the problem by introducing additional procedures. It's specifically pointed to the following. As a result of the small charge and the higher cost of issuing textbooks in the language and letter of national minorities, it is inevitable that the state budget participates in co-financing of their costs and, in practice, seems to be regulated by law for many years, this problem should be complemented in this respect by Article 4 of the Law governing the financing of the purchase of textbooks by means of State budget funds. The submission of applications for the approval of textbooks for schools in the language and letter of national minorities that are only translations of textbooks originally provided in Croatian should be simplified. Namely, since these textbooks as Croatian editions have passed all the approval procedures provided for by the law, there is no reason for their translation into the language and letter of the national minority to repeat the whole procedure once again, but only the draft should be done eventually, the translation review. When it comes to teaching textbooks in the language and letter of national minorities imported from the country of origin, it is not a key problem for them to approve for which the proposed amendments and amendments to the law are without reason introduced by a highly restrictive regime rather than by a whole series of other problems which are not addressed by this proposal of the law. Thus, even if the right to use textbooks from the country of origin is established by the Law on Education and Education in the Language and Letter of National Minorities, it is precisely written that this is possible of course to remain open, open issues such as: importing entities, import procedures, choice of textbooks, financing imports, applicant for authorisation of textbooks and others. As one of the solutions to this problem in the debate, it is proposed to lay down a provision to determine that the ministry will lay down the procedure by special regulation, although we are aware that the specific rules are always subjective because Parliament does not have any authority in them. As regards the provisions of Article 9 The final proposal for a law governing the composition of a committee to determine the consistency of textbooks used in the language and letter of the national minority has expressed the opinion that the proposed provisions are further complicated and unnecessarily accumulated the issue of textbooks for that teaching. All this will extend the process of publishing textbooks in a situation where the lack of textbooks is one of the most difficult problems facing teaching in the language and letter of national minorities. For those textbooks which are expensive because of small fees and specific costs, the establishment of a higher commission whose number and composition are unclearly defined in the proposed provisions is envisaged. That is how the term scientist and expert is used in paragraph 6 of this Act, and in subparagraph 4, the term scientist or expert. It is unclear from the formulation of these provisions whether it is part of the commission to which the methodist enters or does not enter, so it cannot be concluded with certainty how many members of the committee should have two or three. If the proposal were to accept that the issue of importing and approving textbooks from the language and letter of the national minorities should be governed by a special Regulation of the Ministry, that subparagraph 4 would be exceeded. When it comes to a committee for textbooks translated into the language and letter of the national minority in Croatian, all three members of the commission are intended to be teachers or teachers of a particular teaching class working in the language and letter of the national minority. Since this is about textbooks that have already passed the approval procedure for us, it is only important for the translation to be language correct, so the composition of the commission to meet this particular need. Finally, an opinion was expressed when it comes to Article 15. The final proposal for a law amending Article 13 of the Law should provide that national minorities are not subject to the deadlines laid down in Articles 6, 15 and 16 of the Law but should not be excluded from the other provisions of Article 15. It is also pointed out that this provision does not provide for cases relating to copy 5 and so on under Article 5(6) of the Law as governed by the applicable law. The legal provision requires that, with the request for the authorisation of textbooks, the proof that he is a publisher is important in the Republic of Croatia. School institutions with language and letter teaching of national minorities under the law may use textbooks from the country of origin with the approval of the ministry, but since the publishers of these textbooks are outside the Republic of Croatia, with the request for approval of such textbooks, no evidence can be provided that the publisher is registered in the Republic of Croatia and this is a kind of absurd. It has also been warned of the term of students with special needs used in this law. It's said to be unclear who it refers to. In some other documents, the Pedagogical Standard includes children with developmental difficulties, but also extremely talented children. If you think about children with developmental difficulties, then that term should also be used to avoid confusion. The opinion is given that Article 9(6), sub-paragraph 6 of the Rules Committee, should include a professional associate, psychologist, pedagogical or defectologist with experience in working with those children for children. Finally, in Article 15 of the final draft law excluding the application of Article 6(4) and Article 15 of the Law to certain categories of textbooks, including textbooks for children with developmental difficulties, it is proposed to specify that this exception applies only in particularly justified cases to which the Minister decides. Because the automatic application of this provision could make it even more difficult to supply students with developmental difficulties. It is also proposed to maintain the provision of Article 6 of the 15th Act which would allow timely information from parents and students on textbooks to be used in the next school year. In addition to the responsibilities of natural persons who influence teachers, or teachers in selecting textbooks, it is also pointed out that the responsibility of school staff with textbooks is to obtain the opposite benefit. In the vote after the debate, the Committee did not support the draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Teachers for Elementary and High Schools, and by, the Committee was divided, 3 votes to, 2 against and three abstentions. Thank you for your patience.

Acknowledging the populist character of the measure, he took on under his laps and rather self-promotion towards Minister Primorac. But before I go to law school, let me point out a few facts that are important to all of us who want to discuss this issue. Contrary to popular assumptions, teachers and school teachers do not teach the content of textbooks. It is the obligation of teachers and teachers to teach children the content defined in the curricula or tomorrow in the curricula. The textbook is just one of the teaching tools that children and teachers need to be helped, and let too often both moms and dads and grandparents master teaching material. The textbook is a book that differs from some other kind of book, except for the purpose of its content being predetermined by the teaching program, and its appearance and design is the consequence, not only of editorial and authorial creativity, but also of a textbook standard. And in the end, no regulation prescribes that a teacher or teacher in the teaching must use textbooks. In fact, there are developed practices and methodical concepts in the world that do not serve the textbook at all in the learning process in schools. So our laws, and according to the autonomy of teachers and teachers, do not prescribe the obligation to use textbooks. It is only defined that if he chooses to use a textbook, teacher or teacher he must choose the one who has the approval of the ministry. And about this textbook policy itself, to say the same thing, we have to argue here that the years of tampering with inadequately designed state policies towards textbooks, meeting the populist pre-election criteria, and not the professional criteria, have actually brought us into a panic situation today. We are solving problems that would not be at all, nor should they happen as problems, and what this law testifies to is that the state policy of textbooks comes down exclusively to commercial and market regulation. Unfortunately, what the proposal to amend and supplement the Law on textbooks offers is nothing more than a path that is irrevocably dwindling from the knowledge society to the steam society. In addition to the policies already mentioned, giving up the distribution of free textbooks to their children in a law formulated in such a way that all children will receive textbooks, but that some parents will participate in the price, and all the conditions of participation will be prescribed by the Minister by means of a rule by introducing a personal identification number even though these things should be regulated by this law. The law also adopts some newspapers in procedures for the approval of elections and the purchase of textbooks. Let me give you one of the depressions that we find in the SDP quite problematic. The law does not address the issue of ownership of textbooks. When the state bought textbooks to its children and all textbooks, it was logical that the state was also the owners of all these books. Now in a situation where parents will participate to a certain extent in the price of textbooks, it would be logical to predict that they can also become owners of some books at least to the extent that they have participated at their price. If there is no such thing as payment of property, and so far there is no property, we will witness in fact the introduction of a new form of self-increasing or para-taxation which I think neither for this Parliament nor for the stage of democracy development today in Croatian society can and is not acceptable. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23409	Thank you. HNS representative club, Mr. Representative Zeljko Vincelj. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23410	Dear Vice President, representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues. Since 2000, each government has changed the Law on textbooks in each term. Although it was not ideal and was the least problematic law of the coalition government in 2000-2003. The takeover of HDZ authorities took steps to change the law, and most of the changes actually went towards limiting competition and protecting the biggest publishers. All that declaring three main goals. Introducing order on the market i.e. a reduction in the number of textbooks, i.e. publishers. Second, lower prices and higher quality textbooks and third, reduce the weight of school bags. Four years later, it is clear that none of these declared objectives have been achieved. The opposite effects have been achieved. Of course, it is clear to everyone well-intentioned that by limiting competition you cannot achieve higher quality and lower prices than higher prices and stagnating quality. Only sound competition and market action can guarantee an optimal quality price ratio. The law introduced the rule that they could only be three parallel textbook titles per class, which is, of course, the three most representative on the market, with that representation being less than 10%. In practice, the lowest ranked of three first textbooks has more than 20% and it was practically impossible to break into the market with a new title. Enter the first three within two years, unless you have a very significant budget for marketing and dozens of sales representatives processing the market, attend schools. In this way, the market is cemented and the biggest publishers are protected from the competition of smaller, innovative and more flexible publishers. Another even more direct method of protecting large publishers and limiting competition was the regulation of licensing school textbooks in school standard. There it went so far that the prescribed minimum is constantly employed by employees in the development of textbooks. At least 7, although it is a dam that employs a large number of creators, which is one of the basic assumptions for the inventiveness and speed of the response of smaller publishers. Furthermore, it is prescribed that a licensed publisher must own warehouses or have them in the lease, distribution contracts, etc. In a word, anything that can be done to eliminate everyone but the greatest. Since they were so large and protected, the biggest players successfully resisted the requirements for reducing textbook sets, with the aim of reducing the cost and weight of school bags, so the number of components did not decrease, but increased. Workbook, workbook, task collection, curricula, etc. So the result is next. First equally heavy or even harder school bags. Second, the same and even more titles, as smaller publishers were given at the end of the second year and were always teaching again at the approval of a new title that they would then buy for another two years on the market. Third, never higher prices of textbooks that grew significantly faster than inflation. In order for the situation to be even more devastating, the ministry has developed a huge and very expensive bureaucratic apparatus in an attempt to re-regulated the market, and has had to pay all the force of reviewers of members of the judgment committees, develop special software and storage facilities, etc. All these huge funds are as we see spent, without at least achieving any of the declared objectives. The only figure to be handled in public is the total budget spent in the purchase of textbooks, but it would be very interesting to know how much the whole process actually costs, except for the price paid to publishers. In our opinion, it would be much more effective to spend these resources on the continuous improvement and evaluation of teachers. Implementation of a transparent system of monitoring progress to ensure that we have a teacher responsible for the implementation of the curriculum and programmes. Such a teacher certainly will not choose a textbook that makes it difficult for him to implement the program, so all state regulation would become excessive. The task, however, is not easy, so the ministry is significantly opportunised every so much to announce dealing with publishers and introducing order on the textbook market, the populist effect is much faster and cheaper. What are the most important problems of the new bill? The whole proposal for a new law is very problematic, contradictory, locally completely incomprehensible and practically impregnable. Each and every surface reading will reveal a number of problems, so we will look back on only two aspects here. The first aspect that directly promotes corruption and further uncontrolled spending of budget funds. Article 4 states that a teacher chooses a textbook within the limits of a student depending on the type of school, education programme and class, starting with the average cost of making textbooks. It is further prescribed that unused funds can be used by the school to equip i.e. raising the school's pedagogical standard and keeping and using textbooks and other teaching means have to be prescribed by general acts. In such circumstances, the teacher shall be exposed to the Director’s pressure to make his choice exclusively according to the price criteria rather than to the criteria of expertise and quality. Moreover, since at the time of the adoption of the law, we do not know anything about the general acts set out in this Article, the rules on the preservation and use may mean that the Director may put pressure on some textbooks or components, manuals, etc., without buying computers, buildings or the environment, etc. This is very dangerous because it allows for the legal transfer of funds from the Fund for the purchase of textbooks that exists for the sake of declared social sensitivity, the exercise of the right to free education and equal availability of education to all social categories in the funds for the material costs of schools, i.e. Some exclusively operate with the Ministry and the Government sees the information of the health system to construction companies and others. We therefore believe that such a regulation would extend space for legal corruption and uncontrollable spending of budgetary resources. If the objective of saving is declared, why wouldn't the unsecured funds be returned to the budget? It can't be about saving if the funds are poured over to other suppliers. If the ministry considers it necessary to raise pedagogical standards of schools by a number of computers, interactive panels and so on to make it better, more economical and transparent to call a public tender and get a truly best offer. the best price, quantity and quality ratio which is relatively simple when the ministry appears as a large customer in central public procurement. In the proposed school model, they could always buy in a reduced volume and multiple times a year. Several computers, individual contracts with various suppliers and the like, and thus always be below the limit above which they would be required to call a public tender. In particular, it should not be mentioned that the prices that schools would receive in these individual procurements would be very unfavourable. Since there would be no competition among the equal candidates for this purchase, an unrandom area for the corruption of commissions and the like opens up. Schools would now be constantly visited by commercial representatives of computer companies and other potential suppliers, which would certainly not contribute to the integrity of teaching processes in schools. In addition, the biggest publishers would certainly find the means to extend their offer to both IT and other necessary equipment, since they have large sales teams at their disposal anyway. Tens and dozens of MPs who visit schools and principals all the time anyway. Now, along with textbooks, we could offer other equipment as well as appropriate commissions in accordance with the stalking practice. It is perfectly legitimate that the ministry as a customer in public procurement does everything to reduce the purchase price of textbooks but we believe that this should be done by the ministry at the highest level in order not to expose the teacher to a situation in which, depending on the current material needs of the school, it will be possible to put pressure on it to make such or such a choice. The teacher should be put before a choice in which each option will be equally legitimate and indisposed to any pressure. He must choose exclusively according to quality and his professional judgment. This is only possible if the ministry sets prices in agreement with the publishers and then allows the teacher to choose freely from the catalogues of approved textbooks. The second aspect of the law unnecessarily restricts competition and deprives teachers of the quality already achieved in a textbook offer that costs nothing to the state budget. The amended Article 15 states that only one textbook with the highest percentage representation for each teaching course is included in the catalogue in one class of the same education programme. This means that smaller specialized publishers who have developed several different concepts for certain items are once again disabled depending on the representation of different methods, teaching styles and the like. Since different parallel textbooks have their own different users, it is obvious that there is a need for them, and this regulation unnecessarily deny the choice of teachers and thus does not achieve any of the stated objectives. The teacher chooses only one textbook anyway and this has no impact on neither the total cost nor the weight of the school bag. Finally, what makes sense is to throw out a highly represented textbook in order to insert the following from a ranking list that teachers did not give their trust to. It's about the wealth of the offer that the state has in no way contributed to, nor is it what costs. There is no reason to deprive ourselves of options that meet different needs of different types of students aural, visual, kinesthetic, etc. They all require a different approach, so the possibility of a choice where it exists should certainly be welcomed. Here, and finally the findings. The Emperor remained naked, a project of free textbooks for everything will remain recorded as beautiful but for today the obviously unrealistic wish of the Minister of Education. Thank you.

Dear Želimir Janjic, State Secretary at the Ministry of Science, Education and Sports.
ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23543	Thank you very much, dear Vice President of the Croatian Parliament. Dear MPs and MPs before us is the Law on Professional Education in First Reading, let me introduce you to a few details. In the system of secondary education of all students, so somewhere around 186,000 students, 75% are in the system of vocational education, 25% in the system of gymnasium. We have never before had a special law on vocational education, but this matter was governed by two laws. It's the Law on education and education in elementary and high school and it's the Law on crafts. We are now proposing to standardise this with a special law for a number of reasons, so it is fundamental that this system should be regulated as it is specific, since for each economy it is very important and especially in this time it should be flexible to adapt very quickly to the new labour market needs or to continue education in the educational vertical or lifelong learning. Of course, technologies change very quickly, and that's also one of the strong reasons why we need to sort this out in a different way than we've done so far. We have adopted a strategy for developing vocational education in the Republic of Croatia and we are not really the only European countries that want to bring changes to their vocational education precisely for these reasons that I have mentioned. However, when it comes to joining the European Union, we do not have any requirements in this regard, but there is only one article, one provision relating to harmonisation with the acquis of the European Union, that is Article 2 which says that children of citizens of the European Union countries can, under the same conditions, enter vocational education programmes and study with us as our citizens. This provision will enter into force, of course, on the date of the entry into force of the Republic of Croatia into the European Union, and thus it is written in the transitional and final provisions. Of course, this law also relies on other strategic documents such as the development plan for the education and education system from 2005 to 2010 and, of course, also refers to the national programme of measures to introduce compulsory secondary education. This law defines the objectives of vocational education, which include the acquisition of qualifications in the labour market, but also for further education and lifelong learning. What I would particularly like to emphasise is what is important in this law, the establishment of a council on vocational education, so one body that would actually be strategically considering changes in the system of vocational education. What I would particularly like to emphasise, which is important in this law, is the establishment of a Council on Professional Education, so one body that would actually be strategically considering changes in the system of vocational education. That body would be composed of a number of stakeholders referred to this system of vocational education. Furthermore, this law defines the structure and the work of the Professional Education Agency, one part that is not covered by the Regulation establishing the Professional Education Agency, and here we give them some more powers and this is very important. In fact, we established the Professional Education Agency in order to have better support for a system of vocational education that is so large, and before we had only nine advisors covering all these 75%. Educational sectors, 13 in vocational education and each education sector should be provided for by the sectoral council. The Sector Council is designed as a body that will firstly form a competent ministry, so let's say the sectoral council that will cover health programmes, will be appointed by the Minister of Health which is most logical, but of course by all those stakeholders who are addressed to the sector and their tasks will be determined primarily by standards of interest, that is what you mentioned in the previous debate, so the first standards of interest. This will also help the network of programs, so these standards of occupation will be very clear that in some places there the school has no conditions, has not reached that standard in order to educate it. So, this will help the founders to set up a network of programs, so if you want the school networks, as we have established by the past law that we have passed this basic Law on primary and secondary education, and the deadline of a year in which the founders should do so is running. The second thing that will be done by the sectoral council is to establish the standard of qualifications. So these are the outcomes of education, so this is a new paradigm that is not only characteristic of us, but actually for the entire European school system. And ultimately there will be curriculas of individual programmes, individual occupations in certain sectors. And that's the approach we've all advocated, so I dare say everyone here in the Croatian Parliament. Furthermore, these laws and credit points are introduced to be assigned to the course subject of professional qualification. One credit point is the equivalent of 25 hours of burden on average, the average burden on students if they have successfully confirmed acquired competences, and once acquired points of a teaching course are recognised as a acquired part of any other professional qualification consisting of that teaching course, which actually enables both horizontal and vertical passing than has been the case so far. I'll just point out here, so it's about hours, so there might be some different interpretations. The specificities of professional curricula are further prescribed and a minimum share of key to professional competences is defined. A quality assurance system for vocational education is introduced in such a way as to name special committees in schools that are obliged to care for quality with a precise structure as you have seen in the proposal for this law. So, with what we have at the National Centre for the External Evaluation of Education, the implementation of national exams, the external evaluation of schools itself, we have in schools here and these quality teams that will take care of it and therefore, through some self-evaluation process, determine measures, deadlines in which to achieve some of their goals and to remove possibly some of the weak points they have in their work. Conditions for enrolment in secondary vocational education shall be laid down and applications with difficulties shall be subject to registration in accordance with the decision of the competent services. The beneficiary, who for more than two years for legitimate reasons has discontinued his education, is allowed to continue education with the conditions for continuing and acquiring qualifications if the curricula has been changed, and it is possible to be because we predict that we do so every at least five years. So, so far, the question has been raised if he signed up for one program, and he should have ended up doing so. However, if he had stopped his education in the meantime, he would have wanted to finish it, and the program changed what to do now? Here we answer that question. A network of curricula and vocational education institutions proposed by the relevant sectoral councils shall be prescribed. The rights and obligations of the institution for the vocational education of students and employers from the implementation of practical training and exercise shall be laid down by the Treaty on the implementation of practical teaching with the employer, and this procedure shall be laid down by the Minister. Therefore, for each teaching, a contract must be concluded with an employer in crafts. Let's say it's a contract with a licensed craftsman and it's arranged by mutual contractual relations. The right to the prize paid by the employer during the course of practical training and exercise by the employer is determined, and according to the time spent on the practical course, except for some activities where some other regulations are different. Finally, conditions are laid down for the performance of teachers of professional theoretical content, teachers of practical teaching and exercise, professional teachers and co-workers in the course. I will remind you that when we passed the Law on Education and Education in primary and secondary school that we deleted that provision that was restrictive in our system and that she said that with 65 years of age and at least 20 years of retirement must be retired. However, as we talk about the deficit especially of teachers of professional subjects, we removed that provision and accepted that the provision of the general regulation is valid, so the Labour Act, if it is not agreed otherwise with the employer, to overcome this lack of professional staff. In this solution, we are now taking a step further into this, and we say that when it comes to professional subjects, we accept 180 ECTS with addition, as we have so far called, pedagogical-psychological education, which includes also a number of ECTS in those colleges that will give these competences. And in the end, I have to mention the centers of new technologies. We have them, so these are our centres of excellence, we have 16 at this point in the Republic of Croatia. In fact, we commit these centers to ensure that they are trained in those schools that are gravitating this area so that they are fully qualified for their professions that they are educated in those conditions that they may not have in their school, which is very important. Here's Mr. Vice President, for an introduction this much. Thank you to all who will participate in the debate that you will give your contribution, and we will certainly consider it in the preparation of the second reading. So if one of you is interested in me participating in preparing another reading we are open to that possibility. Thank you very much.

The importance of this regulation can be expressed by the fact that professional and artistic schools in Croatia make up three quarters of all secondary schools in Croatia. The importance of the management solution in this area is particularly highlighted. This is primarily the establishment of a Council on Professional Education with broad autonomous areas, and 13 sectoral councils for each educational area. These organisational forms will certainly achieve the cooperation of all interested participants in vocational education. The solutions to improve the position of teachers of professional subjects, as well as the regulatory needs to be established, have also been welcomed. centres of new technologies in vocational education institutions. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23579	Thank you, Mr. Vice President, distinguished MPs and MPs. You have before you the law in first reading, or the proposal for a law on the recognition of foreign professional qualifications. This law is important due to the first row of the extension and closure of Chapter III. in negotiations with the European Union, which is a chapter relating to the right of establishment and the free provision of services. Namely, the essence of this bill is to create a legal framework and administrative infrastructure for the recognition of professional qualifications in the so-called so-called ". regulated professions involving medical professions, all professions relating to the provision of health services. So nurses, medical technicians, midwives, then veterinarians, dentists, pharmacists and architects. In principle, it does not apply to other professions and does not apply to seamen regulated by another special law. In the process of joining the European Union, we must take over certain legal gains of the European Union, which is regulated by Directive 36 of 2005 in the event of the recognition of professional qualifications. So far, we have recognised qualifications in the Republic of Croatia based on the Law on the Recognition of Foreign Educational Qualifications, which only formally recognised the acquired diploma. In principle, the recognition of someone’s education was about the process of recognising identity and identity, which in principle meant that if you had completed a medical college outside the borders of the Republic of Croatia it meant that we would recognise such a diploma for the purpose of continuing education, while to carry out medical activities it was necessary to take a whole series of other steps in which many and professional associations, but other ministries could conduct some procedures separately with a sometimes questionable outcome. In this case, the law will also deal with the recognition in one procedure of these professional qualifications to those involved in regulated professions. Therefore, there is a way, procedure and place of application for recognition of such a qualification of foreign nationals, i.e. nationals of Member States of the European Union. And one includes someone who wants to temporarily or permanently carry out some of the activities in these professions must allow the recognition of his qualifications for the purpose of carrying out such activities within the borders of our country. It should also be pointed out that this law will be relied upon by a whole series of other legal and sublegal acts of other ministries, primarily the Ministry of Health, the Ministry of Agriculture and the Ministry of Civil Engineering, since architects are also one of the regulated professions. The law also respected the comments and opinions we received from the European Commission, and we are aware that in nomotechnical terms, there was simply not enough time in the discussions with certain administrative structures of the European Commission to convince them that some of our expressions are exactly what they are looking for and that is why we have suggested that the law goes into two readings so that until the next session of the Parliament we could fix the text law to be in accordance with our legal tradition. Thank you very much.

The proposed law provides for professional geodetics, chamber association, strict responsibility of authorised geodesy engineer, obligations and means of passing an expert examination, compulsory liability insurance, and supervision of the implementation of this law and regulations adopted under this law. It was concluded that, unlike the applicable regulation proposed by law, overall geodetic activity is regulated in one place. Of course, Croatia's accession procedure also requires Croatia to comply with certain European legislation directives ensuring, among other things, the freedom to carry out geodetic activity on the common market. Only harmonised data from catasters and land books with a real situation on the ground guarantee citizens security in the legal traffic of real estate and provide local self-government and state as a whole with conditions for quality space management. This resulted in the creation of strong private companies as well as the simultaneous departure of experts from the state service. The greatest reason for this is more than modest public service wages. This fact causes negative selection of public service personnel and, of course, the problem of the functioning of the public geodetic service. Then, the Croatian Geodetic Institute as an institution created in 2001, with one of the main objectives of quality control of geodetic products, the system does not use optimally. For example, keep it out of the control of certain products such as cadastrian measurements, so it does not develop with the dynamics needed by such an institution to meet the growing requirements of the profession as well as society as a whole. Finally, the teaching programme of the Geodetic Faculty should be changed in line with the technological changes in the world and with the concept and meaning of spatial information in society. In solving these and other problems, geodetic activity plays a key role and responsibility for the State Geodetic Authority. The fact is that Croatia has since 2003 implemented the National Programme for Land Books and Catastra with a shortened name, a country which is already able to process faster and simpler, register real estate and rights over them, spotlessly obtain information from catasters and land books, and possible verification of digitised data on the Internet. The plan was to coordinate through a 15-year period using digital ortho photo maps, lectorizing catastrian plans and other advanced processes with data from land-book and cadastre systems and guarantee that they match the real state. We believe that the rapid progress in the geodetic profession of recent years and the tendency of European funds to finance actions in the field of catastra and land books is sufficient as a basis for the correction of the length of this period and that citizens, businesses and local communities will not need to wait 2018 to realise this objective. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23611	Dear Vice President, Prime Minister, colleagues and colleagues. The organisation of the Croatian system, spatial planning and construction according to European guidelines began last year with the adoption of the Law on Spatial Planning and Construction, and was continued with the recent adoption of the Law on Construction Products. However, even after the adoption of the Law on Spatial Planning and Construction, which was laid down outside the power of two laws, the 2003 Construction Act and the '94 Act. The provisions of these laws, which regulate the issues of performing spatial planning and designing of professional supervision of construction and construction activities, are in force. These two laws also regulate the Law on the Croatian Chamber of Architects and Engineers in Construction from '98. So at this point, the problem of performing architectural and engineering affairs and activities in spatial planning and construction is regulated through 3 laws. The bill we're discussing today. These jobs will be arranged by law, which certainly is a welcome fact. What this does besides bringing together the problems of the three laws so far is passed by the Law on Architecture and Engineering and Activities in Spatial Planning and Construction. It brings a new way of professional association between authorised engineers and architects of engineers, so instead of one chamber, Croatian chamber of architects and engineers in construction we will get 4 separate chambers following this law. Croatian Chamber of Architects, Croatian Chamber of Engineering, Croatian Chamber of Engineers of Engineering and Croatian Chamber of Electrical Engineering, and the Croatian Chamber of Geodesy Engineers whose constitution is governed by the Law on the conduct of geodetic activity. So, a charming, common society of various engineers will now have the opportunity to ensure better protection of their exact, professional and public interest related to individual engineering interests. The novelty of the existing regulation is that for the first time it is regulated to carry out the activities of the construction project with the purpose of enabling the investor to protect their money and interest, namely successful and legitimate construction. The person carrying out the project management activity of a building cannot at the same time be a designer of this building or carry out professional supervision of its construction. High sentences for architects, engineers, contractors and construction project managers are also provided if they are wrong about the law and profession, thereby causing damage to the investor. Moreover, the investor also has protection at the Institute which is provided for by law, which is the duty of ensuring all participants in the process of designing and building responsibility for the damage they could do to the investor consciously, unconsciously, out of ignorance or failure. Such liability insurance in the performance of its activity may be concluded in classical insurance houses, but may also be directed by each individual engineering chamber. Of course, as with all the laws that we comply with European legislation, this law regulates the way in which these businesses are done by entities from the Member States of the European Union. Although the general finding of interested shareholders in the area of construction and professional associations and trade unions has been carried out in the process of creating a bill of quality and broad public debate with a significant degree of acceptance of their proposals and requirements, there is one conflicting area here between the profession and the legislator. It is about the famous licensing of construction companies to do works in construction, i.e. the difference in access to domestic and foreign contractors. While domestic builders must pass a demanding process of obtaining the necessary license to be able to carry out their activities, performers from the European Union will not need to seek Croatian licensing, but will be able to build in Croatia everything they can according to the regulations of the country in which they are established. The domestic builders consider this discount to be a failure of the legislator and their discrimination against foreign performers. The attitude is therefore that they too would have to be licensed as all domestic companies. In fact, the facts suggest that most European countries do not have a similar licensing system at all. It is not Austria, Germany, Ireland, Denmark, the Netherlands, Slovakia, Sweden, Switzerland, Great Britain. In Belgium, Greece, Spain and Poland, the licence is only required for participation in public procurement operations, and the only criterion for obtaining it is the financial force of the company, not the number of employees as in Croatia. In Portugal, Italy, Norway and France, licences are required for all works in construction, but the criterion is also not the number of employees, but financial forces and technical qualifications. In view of this unequal European company licensing practice, we also see domestic builders as having ambition and desire to work and earn money for Croatian domestic buildings as well as having to commit to an identical licensing process as well as our domestic ones. We ask the Proposer to explain his views and motives for the diversity of access to the licensing of domestic and foreign contractors. It is absolutely not right for domestic and foreign contractors to start from different starting positions. Despite the above-mentioned newspapers and different solutions to design and construction activities in this law, the essence and most significant part of the law is the problem of licensing construction firms to carry out their activities. As is known, this issue is regulated by the regulation in the licensing of construction firms that entered into force on 1 April of this year and managed to argue with all stakeholders: domestic and foreign contractors, professional associations and trade unions with the Ministry of Environmental Protection, Spatial Planning and Construction, which was adopted by this Regulation. Based on their reasoned remarks and concerns that the inadequate regulation will destroy a significant part of construction in Croatia, Minister Dropulic admits after several months that she has made a mistake, so the disputed licensing provisions correct this law that we are discussing today. The basic criticism and bitterness of the contested regulation was that he was suffocating small entrepreneurs and favouring large companies. According to some information in Croatia, the total of cca 130,000 construction workers. In early April, only about 357 were licensed and employed by 26,000, 700 workers, so only a quarter of all employees. The ministry claims that the licensed employs 70,000 workers so far, which means that the rules could leave 60,000 people out of work. We remind you, the Licensing Regulation for the construction work of the Ministry of Environmental Protection, Spatial Planning and Construction has been adopted completely unprofessionally because it is based only on the square of buildings that are being built and on the number of workers needed to build those squares. Of course, there's a lot of illogicalness in that, even the funny ones, for example. A building of 800 square meters can be built by a company that has 40 employees, while only 1 square meter larger building can be built by a company that has 120 and more employees. This only pleases large companies that will get bigger jobs, or they will hire smaller companies and take advantage of profits, which all together leads to the acquisition of construction. It is not in the interest of most Croatian builders, building investors and ultimately Croatian citizens. It is not in the interest of the Croatian state that twenty companies perform 95% of construction jobs. According to that Regulation, 500 workers should also be employed in order to be licensed for the most demanding public purpose structures. This could only satisfy 14 construction companies in Croatia. Under this law, the most demanding public purposes are the highest of 9 license categories, but the criterion is the agreed value of construction over 7m euros and 300 workers are required to be employed for the licence in that category. For building a building of the same value, but which have no public purpose, companies with 200 employees, including 10 authorised building engineers, will be able to license themselves. For each further category and lower work value, fewer and fewer employees are required. The 7th category refers to the least demanding buildings that must not cost more than 1.5m euros, and for them it is enough to have 15 workers. The 8th and 9th categories include the implementation of the simplest buildings that require 8 and 2 employees, respectively. Without those companies that are real ficuses, the average construction company has 30 to 40 employees, and with that number they could do very few jobs. According to this law, the average company can still build an average building, not that it needs 120 workers. For buildings whose construction gross surface is not more than 400 square meters and for buildings to carry out agricultural activity whose gross area is not greater than 600 square meters, no licence is required. However, the law added that up to 400m euros should not be worth more than 150m euros. The general condition for all licensing categories is that in every company that deals with construction, as many as 3/4 employees, there must be professional staff, i.e. architectural, construction, engineering, electrical or geodetic professions. The licence shall be lost if contributions are not paid to employees of pension and health insurance. The employee of the contractor must be employed indefinitely in full-time on construction operations, except that the employees of the contractor from the quota of foreign nationals can be employed for a specified period of time under a specific law. We remind one of the basic declared intentions of the Ministry of Environmental Protection, Spatial Planning and Construction in the adoption of the disputed regulation was to virtually eliminate them, or eliminate them from the possibility of obtaining the job of an engineering company without employees of construction workers, etc. the ficus of companies that were getting jobs and then reselling it to qualified performers. With this law, the company remains in the game of getting jobs to run a construction project that the investor can and does not have to negotiate. Here's the impression that the proposed solutions in this law are mostly satisfied. It is essential to abolish the wrongly restrictive regulation on the licensing of the Ministry of Environmental Protection, Spatial Planning and Construction and to allow for a fairer distribution of potential in the performance of construction works, and the company's ficus can survive on the market if it will further employ experts who can lead construction projects on behalf of investors. I'm closing up. The Croatian People's Party club will support the adoption of this law.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23613	Dear Mr. Vice-President of the Croatian Parliament, Mr. Secretary of State, distinguished colleagues and fellow MPs and MPs. In front of us is the Final Proposal for the Law on Architectural and Engineering Affairs and Activities in Spatial Planning, which continues to reform the Croatian system of spatial planning and construction. The reform should at least bring about a positive change in principle, but it can be known to cause a deterioration of the situation. When adopting laws in the area of spatial planning, basic objectives should certainly be protection and prudent management or management of space as our most valuable resource, then moving to a quality if possible sustainable building in space, as well as a simpler and more transparent procedure for the establishment of building permits. The same goals should have this law. As we have already pointed out in our first reading, we are not unfortunately sure whether or not this bill will contribute to these goals. According to the explanation of this final proposal in the area of spatial planning, several amendments have been made, such as insurance, multidisciplinary and quality in the design of spatial plans, including representatives of different professions such as technical, biotechnical, natural, social, etc. I'm in the process of making it. Although this change is likely to improve the quality of spatial design we welcome as a good move. We believe that this in the way it is stated is not a guarantee for achieving the most important objective, and this is protection and thoughtful management of space. The multidisciplinaryity of urban planning, i.e. spatial planning, has been highlighted both through the knowledge of architectural and construction profession and through the repetition of the social and economic needs of a particular area and other professions such as geography, biology, ecology, demographics, biodynamic development, etc. Urbanism differs significantly from the architectural profession in its part-round and objectives. Since the urban profession is much wider than architectural and even their goals can be in great conflict. While the architectural profession mainly seeks to build more intensively in space, the urban, or spatial-plannery profession tends to establish harmony and balance between building and developing economic potentials on the one hand, and to preserve space and natural resources as the most valuable wealth of the community on the other. With this in mind, we are afraid that favoring the architectural profession at the expense of the urban, or spatial and planic professions can have extremely great negative consequences for the situation in Croatia, especially in the Croatian coast. Despite the inclusion of other spatial planning professions, it cannot be noticed that in other parts of this law, in particular in the field of design and professional construction supervision, it is also expressed to favour the architectural and construction profession in relation to all other technical professions that must inevitably be involved in the business under this law. That is why this bill has more illogical and questionableness, because it has the fact that it has been created according to measure and in the interest of the architectural and construction profession, and in some parts it is seen that it is also created for the narrow part of the entities involved in the business within that profession. For example, Article 51 provides that a legal person to carry out the activities of the management of a full-time sea construction project and for an indefinite period of time must have at least one employed master of law and a master of economy with at least five years of work experience in the profession. At the same time, graduate engineers are sufficient for the field of architecture and other technical professions for the same activity. The question is, how many legal persons in Croatia who are involved in design work have in their composition employees in academic called master's or more. College, or university diploma of a particular direction, is mostly a satisfactory condition for performing professional and responsible business in the area of the economy, and academic titles are mostly prescribed as an obligation in the fields of scientific, higher education, etc. an institution. Therefore, the question arises whether this article is also created in accordance with the model of a particular institution, or whether this article is intended to exclude some legal persons from the ability to manage projects. Another far more drastic example of favouring the architectural and construction profession concerns the position of the applicant in relation to the engineering profession or experts in the area. In this respect, the problem part of this law is the area of design, more specifically, of Chapter VI. The task of performing project design and control activities is regulated, and the engineering profession is significantly underrated. Article 60 of the final draft law excludes engineering engineers from the possibility of producing a project of load-bearing machinery structures. This is, and is generally known, the work done by engineering engineers with a professional exam, therefore authorised for design work, so far very professionally, responsibly and successfully carried out. A number of references are made to engineering and steel construction projects mainly in the area of process and industrial plants both in the country and abroad, which are evidence that engineers know and can work on a very high and demanding level of design. Many designers have authorized shipbuilding engineers, their entire working life, and this means for ten years they have designed projects of various steel structures. Some of these projects, such as liquid tanks, volume of several million litres, different containers of grain materials, pressure vessels, industrial chimneys, carrying structures of industrial and energy plants, pipelines for the transport of different products such as pipelines, pipelines, steam pipelines, smokeworks and similar facilities, function without any problem for years. So they're designed and they're made in quality. Despite all this, with this bill of law, the designers who designed them can no longer do it or may not be able to do it. The proposer of this law is the Ministry of Environmental Protection, Spatial Planning and Construction. It has decided so voluntarily without relevant arguments, assessing and undermining the engineering profession. To this course of action in connection with the problem described, the entire profession in the field of engineering responded. The fact is that, during the preparation of this proposal, the applicant received a clear and expressed negative opinion of the engineering profession as regards the restrictions on the implementation of these tasks, which were prescribed in Article 60, i.e. in the previous proposal, Article 67, but did not find it appropriate to consider it more seriously, you can see that it is manifested or respected. The class of engineering engineers of the Croatian Chamber of Engineers in Construction has repeatedly pointed to the illogicality and harmfulness of such an approach by the promoter in the legal definition of these jobs. The most important Croatian institutions for the education of engineering engineers, four universities, the Faculty of Engineering and Shipbuilding from Zagreb, the Faculty of Electrical Engineering and Shipbuilding from Split, the Faculty of Engineering from Slavonski Brod and the Faculty of Engineering from Rijeka were jointly manifested about the position of the promoters of this law, i.e. the Ministry of Environmental Protection, Spatial Planning and Construction. Argumented in this respect, confirming that engineering engineers fully meet the conditions for performing design and professional supervision of engineering steel structures. The joint display of the above-mentioned colleges, which the applicant received in due time, highlighted the inappropriate depravation of the right to work by authorised engineers of engineering. Unfortunately, to multiple official correspondence, the proponent of this law did not react or appear. He did not respect any of the arguments presented and remains on his original proposal, which made it impossible for the engineering industry to carry out design and control tasks of engineering construction projects. This bill is marked P.Z.E. it would therefore be in line with the laws of the European Union. Or as the usual is said, it should be a European law to be applied in Croatia. But what kind of European law makes it impossible for engineers to carry out a certain type of professional work that I repeat is absolutely educated and trained in their own country, and that they can do the same in other European countries. The answer to this question obviously knows only the legislator. Until such an answer is given to us, justified by profession, and not by interests, we will not be able to support the adoption of this law. And it's in this kind of proposed form. I must say, that is precisely why we have proposed a few amendments to which we wish to improve this law which could be done and corrected by the draft of this law. Whether or not they will be respected will depend on any change of attitude. Thank you very much.
