ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23615	Dear Vice President, representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues. After the first reading of the bill on architectural and engineering affairs and activities in spatial order and construction before us, the same bill is in a changed form. We welcome that positive change. In... the article remained unchanged. Here I am referring to Article 51 which speaks of the following: "The activities of the construction project can be carried out by a legal person registered for the purpose of performing these tasks which, in full-time, has employed at least one project manager, an authorised architect who has at least 5 years of working experience in the profession, an authorised engineer of construction, engineering and electrical engineering with at least 5 years of working experience in the profession, a Master of Law with at least 5 years of working experience in the profession and a Master of Economics with at least 5 years of working experience in the profession". I believe that this article should also be amended and that it reads: "The activities of the construction project can be carried out by a legal person registered to carry out these jobs which, in full-time, has at least one project manager employed", and I will explain why. There are several reasons for this attitude. The first lies in the fact that no project is the same in construction. The project team that will manage the construction project of Zagreb Airport will certainly not be the same as those who will eventually build the Pelješki bridge. Both will have architects and engineers of all professions, as well as lawyers and economists but probably not the same. Because there are no universal experts who can cover all kinds and more complex projects in construction. Today is the time of the specialist. The project manager collects a team of specialists for the specific task when he gets a new task and is disbanded at the end of the job. It is also common that teams are composed of members from around the world. I wonder how it'll satisfy the law then. Another reason is the possibility of abuse of the law to create a monopoly. Large companies satisfy the proposed law in the section of the composition of experts, but do not essentially have a profiled frame that can cover all projects. In this way, they will get jobs, yet they will take external specialists for individual areas, as they would have taken if there were no such provision. A good example of this is the negotiation of a public private partnership project. The third reason is the possibility of association provided by the existing Public Procurement Act. It enables social positions to be associated with the purpose of building project teams that will carry out a specific task in the best interests and legal interest and according to investor requirements. The next reason is that this treatment of project management activities, construction in the Member States of the European Union, is not available. And the last reason, but not less important, is that architects and engineers who are not involved in the management of construction projects, but only in spatial planning, designing and supervision are in a much more favourable position because for the establishment of their companies they must only have themselves as a minimum to start their activities, which is no less complex than the activities of building projects. The attached proposal to amend Article 51 is written in affirmative tone and relies on a similar formulation as set out in Article 14 of the same law, and provides for the possibility of equating the position of spatial plan makers and project leaders in a business that requires a multidisciplinary approach. It would be good if what applies to architects and legal persons who draw up plans, also applies to project leaders and legal persons involved in the project management activities in construction. Otherwise, if this proposal is not adopted, it will become evident that this law is being prepared for large companies, such as IGH "Invest engineering" and so on. to take over projects from small companies. Also in the first reading of the bill, we have assessed that, apart from architects and construction engineers, project leaders can also be engineers of other professions, does not follow the European tradition, and poses a potential risk of monopolising one part of the market. And at the end of my presentation, I would like to refer to Minister Dropulić's statement at the first reading of this law, and then Minister Dropulić said this, quote: "When engineers are at their university or shipbuilding engineers studying or listening to the subject of static, which speaks of how to design the subject of static that speaks of how to design a carrying steel construction of a hall, then we will certainly enter it into the law." Here, if Mrs. Minister doesn't know what the engineers are studying in college, I'll just list some of the items and these are mechanics or statics. Statistics is the basic and simplest part of the mechanics. And in mechanics is taught about the mechanics of stiff bodies, of steel, of frame... Carriers, spatial structures, mechanical structures, including steel aluminium and bearing structures in the case, metal structures for which the minister claims that the engineers do not learn about them. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] With such a statement, the minister insulted all the students of engineering colleges, all the professors and deans at those universities, and it would be nice to apologize to them. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23617	Mr. President, Mr. Secretary of State. The adoption of the Law on Spatial Planning and Construction, which entered into force on 1 October 2007, launched a reform of the existing Croatian system of spatial planning and construction, which was found to be suitable for the initial transition phase from which the Republic of Croatia came out and that it is necessary to change it in order to bring the systems of the Member States of the European Union further closer. The reform was continued with the recent adoption of the law on construction products and should have ended at the legislative level by adopting the proposed Law on Architecture and Engineering Affairs, on spatial planning and construction. At present, the issue of performing architectural, engineering and spatial planning and construction activities. the supply of jobs of persons professionally engaged in construction and various organisational forms in which these tasks are carried out shall be governed by the provisions of Article 3(3) of the Law. The provisions of the mentioned laws on the implementation of architectural, engineering and spatial planning and construction activities by modelling the European Union countries, however, the implementation of those acts in the part relating to the performance of these tasks in the Republic of Croatia by entities from the Member States of the European Union is not in line with the European Community Directive on the recognition of professional qualifications and the European Community's Internal Market Services Directive. Also, the way these issues are being regulated today does not respect the Croatian qualification framework that corresponds to the European qualification framework adopted at the end of 2007. Furthermore, the provisions of the mentioned laws on issues related to the performance of architectural and engineering activities and activities in spatial planning and construction are not arranged in an optimal manner. This relates first of all to the professional association of authorised architects and engineers who are assessed to be better organised in order to protect their but also public interest through more professional associations or chambers than in a single chamber, that is, the Croatian Chamber of Architects and Engineers in Construction. This law also regulates the implementation of administrative and other procedures, as well as the rights and obligations of the authorities of the state administration, legal and natural persons in relation to that body. To this end, the proposed law regulates the professional activities of spatial planning, designing and professional construction supervision, organisational forms of doing business, carrying out construction activities, carrying out the activities of the construction project. Foreign persons performing the tasks of drawing up implementing documents of spatial arrangement, association in the chamber, compulsory liability insurance, supervision of law enforcement and subsequent provisions. The adoption of this law will obviously make much more order in this branch of the economy, and given the growing above, it will support the adoption of this law. Thank you.

In its opinion, it is proposed that, in view of the exceptional importance for the survival and operation of the freshwater aqua sector, it is necessary to add, in Article 5 of this proposal, the compensation for the use of water for the production of fish in closed waters. Some board members agreed with the proposals of the Croatian Chamber of Commerce as these proposed solutions could help a weakened branch of freshwater fisheries, which is burdened with high water fees and damages from fish birds. In the debate further raised doubts as to solutions which are charged for the use of water for affected quantities of water as opposed to the previous quantities of water delivered. In this respect, it is also intended to finance the recovery of the loss of a particular service area, which can significantly affect the rise in water prices. In fact, it is questionable whether the transitional period of 5 years is realistic given the state in which the waters are located. It also draws attention to the fact that bonds which release wastewater and pay compensation for the protection of water by the amount of water released in implementation clearly determine the correction coefficient which reduces the amount of compensation in the areas in which the waste water treatment device is built. It is also argued that the questionable solution is that producers and importers of mineral fertilisers from plant protection products pay two-base water protection fee as well as persons who release waste water and as producers, or importers. On the basis of the debate held, the Committee on Regional Development, Forestry and the Water Economy of the Croatian Parliament unanimously decided to propose to the Croatian Parliament to reach the next conclusion, which is that the proposal for a law on the financing of the water economy and any observations and proposals made in the debate will be submitted to the applicant in order to prepare the final draft law.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23659	Mr. President, Secretary of State, colleagues and fellow MPs. The adoption of a new Water Economy Financing Act, as stated by the applicant, is primarily necessary in order to bring the legislation in the water economy into line with European Union legislation, and to ensure sufficient resources to ensure water protection in order to achieve the good condition of all water in water areas. This proposal retains all existing water compensations and this water contribution, water use fees, water protection fees, water control fees, sand and gravel extraction fees, Melioration discharge fees and irrigation fees, but has been partially supplemented by both the purposes and the partially modified method of accounting. In addition to the fees referred to above, two more are introduced by the law, although they have been taken over by the Law on the Communist Economy and this compensation for development and accession fees. Development fee can be introduced, and is used to build public water supply buildings and public draining and water treatment buildings. It shall be calculated by means of a water service and community income account of the service area, while the connection fee may be introduced for the connection of buildings and other properties to public water supply and public sewage, waste water, and the unit of local self-government whose income is also used for the same purposes as the development fee. At the very beginning, I would like to say that, despite the fact that there are 9 types of compensations provided for as sources of funding for the water economy, and that other sources of funding and this state budget, as well as the budget of units of regional self-government, units of local self-government are provided. It's about a lot of resources. Investments in the water economy are really too small. The problem of the water economy is primarily an unsatisfactory level of population involvement in public water supply systems and public sewage systems, large water losses in the water supply system are insufficient use of water potential and insufficient flood protection. If that were true, that's what the following data is about. The connection to public water supply systems is 76%. The integration of citizens and the economy to public sewage systems is 40%, and of which 23% of wastewater is purified by the population collected by the public sewage system. Croatia needs large investments in the conservation of drinking water springs, the recovery of pollutants, the prevention of the release of polluted water into the environment, further construction, reconstruction and regular maintenance of water structures, flood water protection systems, as well as the deforestation systems of excess surface and groundwater. Furthermore, the damage to agriculture, the water economy and other activities due to the occasional occurrence of excess water and floods or droughts are almost regular occurrences with increasingly severe consequences. Agriculture as well as other activities related to soil management as a national treasure is the most affected. As a result, it is necessary to carry out the water treatment measures as well as irrigation because flood risks today are very high and not only because of the insufficient construction of the flood protection system and the drain system, but also more due to insufficient maintenance of the implemented systems. The question arises in the purpose of spending the funds collected, and the conditional and justification of the existence of so many types of compensation. I will mention here the water contribution that was introduced during 2006 and it seems that the Croatian Waters' revenue has been very generous because, according to the promoter, sufficient resources have been provided over the past 2.5 years for the intended purposes financed from this contribution, so the construction of regulatory water protection structures and water contribution funds is foreseen instead of State budget funds. I will remind you, the water contribution pays by the investor, is paid in 3 zones, and the base consists of business premises, residential buildings for permanent and occasional housing, social standard facilities, religious facilities, production facilities, roads, product pipelines, telecommunications sewers, open special buildings and other open buildings. The division of the three zones itself had no reasonable explanation because they were under other areas of Croatia, and this zone B entered all areas except the City of Zagreb and the protected coastal area of the sea and the area of special state care. Thus, the same water contribution was paid by the investor in the smallest place, e.g. The municipality of Crnac, located in Virovitica-Podravska County as well as the one in the centre of Osijek. The SDP considers it necessary to examine the justification of the existence of a water contribution, i.e. we believe that its abolition is necessary because it would thus reduce the contributions paid by economic operators to investment, and in the coming crisis, the disbursement of investors would mean the provision of new jobs. The Government should also review the eligibility of the water compensation for the freshwater aquaculture sector, i.e. we propose that that sector be exempt from the water compensation payments set out in Article 5 of this Act. We believe that this is necessary because of the specificity of this production which the water uses only as a medium for cultivation and returns it back to the repient from which it was taken in the same or even better condition. In addition, we say that there are no such water charges in the countries of the European Union which are directly competitors in the production of freshwater fish. I would also like to say something further on the compensation for the melioration drain paid on agricultural land in the area of the regional self-government unit in which the buildings were built for the detailed Melioration Disposal owned by those units. This fee has not been announced so far because the system of detailed melioration disposal over the past 10 years has been devastated, abandoned and first needed to be brought into a functional state. However, the assessment of the arrangement of almost 40% of the system of detailed melioration disposal that has been brought to the function is very superficial and casual and according to my knowledge too high. Furthermore, in accordance with the new Water Law Proposal, units of the district self-government will take over trained systems for further care. Management and maintenance, which will be financed from this fee for which they are due, shall also collect funds to ensure regular maintenance. I must say, this provision opens up a whole series of questions. What is meant by the takeover of qualified systems prescribed in Article 94 of this proposal? Does this mean channels brought into zero state functionally or channels with solved property-legal issues? Namely, no property-legal relations have been resolved for a number of channels. Is this going to solve Croatian waters before the handover or regional self-government after the takeover since it is a significant means for geodesic-parceleration elaborates? There are a lot of channels that are not even listed in the cadastrian plans, and the problem of buying-out is the kind of privileges that need to be implemented because some channels are owned by natural or legal persons. That is, the problem is unsettled property-legal relations. In addition, in the case of resolved property relations for the canals of detailed disposal, a book was held in such a way that the name of the county was entered in the owner's register for the management of Croatian Waters. We're asking what the management of the district self-government units means. By transferring these jobs to units of the district self-government, it means establishing new services, i.e. new cost for employees’ salaries, providing space, because someone must carry out these tasks that are put into the jurisdiction of local units. Then the question arises whether the Melioration discharge fee applies only to the open canal network of detailed drains, and what about the drenated surfaces? They don't mention each other anywhere. Who manages them, and who should maintain them? In addition to the problem of taking over a trained channel network, there is another uncertainty, which is Article 94(2), which prescribes that revenue determined by decision on the amount of compensation cannot be greater than the amount of funding needed for the financing of the building maintenance programme of a detailed meliorative discharge. How exactly does it predict the means needed, or what if they are smaller or if they are larger? In addition, these funds are also financed by the operating expenditure to achieve the very revenue from the compensation, that is, Article 51(2) of this proposal. The applicant under point 2 of the assessment of the situation and the basic issues to be resolved by law only speaks of the obligations of the debtor of the compensation, which is one-way, which is, with the right of the obligor, compensation for the damages resulting from the construction and regular technical and economic maintenance of the water flow, i.e. regulatory and protective water structures. Which, with the rights of the debtor, is the damage caused by the flood or the non-delivery of drinking water to public water supply buildings, for example, by the fact that it was in Slavonski Brod or other damages incurred under the management of the users of the compensation financed under this law. In addition, when determining the level of the Melioration discharge fee, it should be taken into account that it is corrected by a coefficient based on a catastrian class one to seven and a single cadastrian culture, e.g. Oranica, vineyard, orchard and the like. As regards the compensation for irrigation paid on agricultural land which is irrigated in the ownership of units of the regional self-government and which has not been set up so far, however, the implementation of the national irrigation and management programme of agricultural land and water, as the proposer said, created conditions for the delivery of water for water to end-users, for the irrigation of 15 thousand hectares of land. We have to say that it's in the government's 2008/2011 programme. indicated that at least 60 thousand hectares of land would be irrigated. To date, it is visible from the applicant's data, only 15 thousand hectares are irrigated, which means that the project itself is progressing with a snail's step. It is tragic that Croatia, when water is used for irrigation, stands at the very bottom of the scale, is irrigated 1% of European countries, 1%, while in other European countries, e.g. For example, Italy, Bulgaria, Romania, is irrigated around 20.30%, Albania is over 50% of the agricultural area. Due to the slow progress of the irrigation project to be financed from state sources, pre-accession funds, global banks and others, large drought damages were particularly known in 2001, '03, and 2005, where damages were estimated in billions of kuna. I have to say here that in the county from which I come Virovitica-Podravska two pilot irrigation projects are being implemented and that the activities around this first project that started in 2005 are only signed at 11 months this year, so around three years, property legal issues, protected belt problems and others are being resolved. Because of the slowness of the entire national project, peasants are left with nothing but to rely on how God gives. And finally, I want to say something about the development fee, that is, the accession fee. With these fees, citizens pay for the same purpose. Because the revenues made from these fees are used to finance the construction of public water supply buildings and public sewage buildings and the treatment of waste water. Only the revenue from the development fee belongs to the service area community while the income from the accession fee belongs to the local self-government. Thus, it turns out that citizens who pay the accession fee, i.e. those who have already paid it and who are connected to water supply systems or disposal systems, must also pay development fees. Instead of financing construction, it is transferred to citizens who thus pay two fees for one and the same purpose. For all the above reasons, the SDP Club will not support this bill unless the applicant accepts our comments and the proposals that I made in this debate. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23665	Mr. Vice President, Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues. The Croatian Democratic Community club will support the proposal of the Water Economy Financing Act. The development and adoption of the Water Economy Financing Act is primarily necessary in order to bring legislation in the water economy into line with European Union legislation and to ensure sufficient resources to ensure that water protection measures are implemented in order to achieve the good state of all water in water areas. The proposal for a Water Economy Financing Act has been retained all existing water compensations, but they have been partially supplemented by the purposes and in part the method of accounting changed. In view of the takeover into account, public water supply and public disposal and wastewater treatment activities in the part-round water economy, two new fees are also prescribed in the proposal of the Water Economy Financing Act. Development and accession fees. The registration and income of both fees has been possible so far in accordance with the existing Law on the municipal economy, however, they were not legally defined, but were prescribed by the operator's will, with the consent of their founders. The same led to an uneven calculation of water prices in the distribution area. The development fee must be used to ensure uniform development in the service area primarily in the development in line with state investment programmes in the construction of public water supply buildings and public draining and waste water treatment. The development championship towards the readiness of the project documentation and the state of property legal relations. The connection fee may be introduced for the connection of buildings and other properties. The water economy funds are used to finance the construction of regulatory and protective buildings, to finance construction of buildings for basic melioration disposal, to finance the implementation of jobs and prevention measures, regular and extraordinary flood defences. Since the past two and a half years have provided sufficient resources for the intended purposes, the financing of the construction of water protection facilities and water contribution funds has been revised instead of State budget funds, which has so far been the case of water-bearing income of Croatian waters. The funds for water treatment will continue to be used to finance the implementation of operations and prevention measures, regular and extraordinary flood defences, construction of building for basic meliorisation disposal and construction of mixed water structures for melioration. The revenue from the water treatment fee is used according to the principles of solidarity and in the water needs championship. Water charges shall be used for the collection and management of water supply data and their use, control of the state of supply of water and take measures for their rational use, water research, water supply insurance, financing of construction of public water supply highways. Water protection charges shall be used to prepare water protection plans and organise their implementation, monitoring and quality of water quality and to take measures to protect them, finance state-of-the-art buildings, public disposal of waste water. For the protection of water, the change is that the basis for the collection of compensation for persons who release waste water, the amount of waste water or persons who affect or deliver water to public water supply buildings in the areas of the settlement without the construction of wastewater disposal and treatment facilities, the amount of water affected, instead of the quantities delivered through the public water supply system. It is also envisaged to systematically implement the principle of wage polluter for scattered pollution sources introduced in the already existing law. However, without providing for sublegal acts to implement it. The funds for gravel and sand compensation are used to finance jobs and measures to prevent flood defence, finance and recovery of water conditions of good water and water buildings created by the illegal extraction of gravel, sand and stone. Funding of maintenance of buildings for detailed Melioritional Disposal of Units of Regional and Regional Self-Government Units. The revenue from the Melioration discharge fee shall be used in accordance with the principle of solidarity and championship in the needs of the regional and regional self-government units. The fee for the melioration drain has not been charged so far and has not been charged, as the system of detailed melioration disposal over the past ten years has been devastrated, abandoned and still partially in mine suspected areas, and originally needed to bring it into functional condition. Since over the past two and a half years almost 40% of the system of detailed drain mellioration has been brought into operation, it is expected to continue regularly. The fee for the melioration disposal and the revenue of the budget of units of local, regional and regional self-government, shall be determined by the assembly of the units of the regional and regional self-government by its decision. A rule which will be provided for in more detail in the calculation and payment of the fee. The irrigation fee is used to finance the maintenance of buildings, to irrigate units of local and regional self-government. The revenue from the irrigation fee shall be used according to the principles of solidarity and championship in the needs of the built-up irrigation system. The irrigation fee has not been set yet, however, by implementing the national irrigation and management programme for agricultural land and water over the past three years, delivery conditions have been created for watering end-users to irrigate approximately 15,000 hectares, which is an increase of almost 67% compared to 2004 when only 9,000 hectares were irrigated. Compensation for irrigation and revenue of the budget of the units of the regional self-government. The bill provides for decentralisation of funding with clear competencies in the national, regional and local sectors. The units of the regional and regional self-government shall, by their decision, determine the amount of compensation for the melioration disposal and irrigation fees, and the resources of the compensations made shall be the revenue of the budget of the units of the regional self-government and shall be used to maintain those buildings. The community of the service area can introduce development compensation, and the same income is the community of the service area and is used to finance the construction of public water supply and wastewater. Finally, the local self-government unit can introduce a connection fee and is the same income as the local self-government unit and is used to finance and build public water supply and disposal and treatment of waste water. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23681	Thank you, Mr. Vice President, Mr. Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues. Any cash expenditure which charges a spatial meter of water supplied to end-users and which is in direct or indirect connection with the protection of its quality and quantity and which is made possible by the construction and management of water infrastructure that enables it to be exploited or released in accordance with an ecologically acceptable standard, shall be the price of water. The prices of water in Croatia range from 2.5 to 16 kuna per metre of cubic water delivered, resulting from different approaches to price formation. Within that price there is a constant fee for the use of water and water protection fees, which together amount to 70 per metre cubic and are equal for all citizens. Water prices must be formed according to the same economic criteria that allow compensation for all maintenance costs, system functioning, necessary development and should not include irrationality in municipal activities and components which are neither directly nor indirectly in the water management function and water services. In the water sector of the Republic of Croatia, as well as the vast majority of European Union countries outside the water price, protection from harmful water action, melioration and meliorative irrigation are financed. The adoption of the Water Economy Financing Act is primarily necessary in order to bring legislation in the water economy into line with European Union legislation and to ensure sufficient resources to ensure the implementation of water protection measures in order to achieve the good state of all water in water areas. The new Act on the financing of the water economy retains all existing water compensations but is partially complemented by the purposes and partly modified accounting methods. The papers proposed by the adoption of this law are development and accession fees. The development fee is used to finance construction of public water supply buildings and public sewage buildings, treatment of waste water. It must be used to ensure uniform development in the service area, the development championship in accordance with state investment programmes in the construction of public water supply buildings and public sewage and wastewater treatment buildings, the development championships according to the degree of preparation of documentation projects and other preconditions for the start of investment in the construction of the system. Where, in order to protect springs in sanitary protection zones, specific increased investment is required in public water supply buildings or public wastewater disposal buildings, the community of the service area may introduce development compensation in the service area to meet these needs. The development fee shall be calculated by means of a water service account and shall be the income of the service area community. A connection fee may be introduced for the connection of buildings and other properties, if the connection of other properties is permitted by general conditions of supply of water services to buildings and public water supply and public disposal of waste water. The investor or owner of the building or other real estate and the connection fee shall be used to finance the construction of public water supply buildings and public sewage buildings and to clean up waste water in the unit of local self-government that makes them available. The opening and income of both fees was possible and so far in accordance with the existing Law on the Communist Economy, however, they were not explicitly legally defined but prescribed by the will of the operatives, with the consent of their founders. The same led to an uneven calculation of water prices in the distribution area. As the reform and establishment of the municipal sector under the new Water Act provides for a transitional period of three years for the award of development and compensation for accession in accordance with the new Water Economy Financing Act is foreseen for a period of 6 months from the establishment of the service areas. The adoption of the Water Economy Financing Act is necessary in view of the introduction of new provisions necessary for the provision of resources to achieve an effective, economic and guaranteed standard of water services in public water supply and disposal and wastewater treatment activities, which have been taken over in accordance with the adopted Water Management Strategy under this law under the responsibility of the Ministry of Environmental Protection, Spatial Planning and Construction. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23685	Thank you very much. Mr. Vice President, Mr. Secretary of State, colleagues and fellow MPs. Here we can see from this part of the debate that it really is two extremely important legislative proposals. It is one that regulates the legal status of water, water goods and water buildings, etc. and the second is the one that regulates the sources of funds for the financing of the water economy’s operations, in particular water compensation, payment obligations and obligors, the basis and method of accounting, the determination of the amount and the purpose of the spending of those funds, the performance and limitation, and other issues related to the achievement and use of those funds. It is therefore a proposal for a law on water and a proposal for a law on the financing of a water economy currently under discussion. The current Water Economy Financing Act was adopted in 1995, so at the same time as the Water Act, which is quite understandable and logical, and during the next period it experienced several changes and additions that were published in the "National Journals" that I do not now list them in order. The principles on which the financing of the water economy is based and under the applicable law, but also under the proposal of the new law, are clearly laid down in Article 3(3). The proposal for a law on the financing of the water economy is based on the basic principle of user pay pollutants. This principle, which is also highlighted in the Water Law proposal, specifically means that funds for the financing of the water economy are provided from water charges paid by water system users, i.e. water pollutants and from the price of water services paid by the users of those services. In accordance with the applicable Act on the financing of the water economy, water financing funds are provided from the State budget from water charges such as water contributions, water use fees, water protection fees, water management fees, sand and gravel drawback fees, Melioration draining and irrigation fees, then the budget of the units of the regional and local self-government and other sources of funds. It is important in this section to mention that the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the 2005 Water Economy Financing Act created assumptions for the adoption of a series of implementing rules that circle the formal legal whole and thus created the preconditions for the regular and stable financing of the water economy. The basic reasons for the adoption of the new Water Economy Financing Act are, on the one hand, in the need to bring national legislation into line with the acquis of the European Union and the need to provide sufficient resources for the implementation of water protection measures with a view to achieving the good condition of all water and in all water areas, on the other hand, due to the reform of the municipal sector provided for in the proposal for a Water Law. The proposed Act on the financing of the water economy establishing the sources of funds for the financing of the water economy and other details relating to their collection and use, which I have already talked about, retains all the sources of funding prescribed by the applicable law and any existing water charges which have only been partially supplemented by the purpose and the partially amended method of accounting. However, due to the takeover of public water supply, public draining and treatment of wastewater in the water economy area, which will take place as part of the reform of the municipal sector provided for in the proposal for a Water Act. The proposal for a Water Economy Financing Act also provides for two new fees. So, development and accession fees. Development fee may be introduced by the service area communities to be organised after the Government of the Republic of Croatia has established the limits of a particular service area in accordance with the proposal of the Water Act and would be paid at the price of public water supply, wastewater disposal and treatment prices. Compensation will be introduced in cases of increased investment needs in public procurement systems, public disposal of waste water, and with the aim of equating, or even development in the entire service area, when it comes to the possibility of using these services and developing fees shall be calculated through the accounts for water services and revenues of the community of the service area which, at the same time, determine the amount of compensation. Next, the accession fee under Article 66 of the Law will be able to introduce units of local self-government for the connection of buildings and other properties to public water supply and public sewage. This fee is the income of units of local self-government whose representative body, by its decision, determines the amount of compensation and how it is calculated and charged. The funds resulting from the accession fee shall be used for the same purposes as the development fee. These two fees could, it is important to stress this strongly, even so far, in accordance with the Law on the municipal economy, but they were not explicitly established, so they were prescribed by the operator's will, of course, with the consent of their founder, which led to an unequal calculation of water prices in certain distribution areas. Since the provision of the law, the new Water Law which regulates this area, and due to the high requirements foreseen by the reform of the municipal sector, enter into force only on 1 January 2012. Therefore, a transitional period of 3 years is foreseen. The new Water Economy Financing Act set 30 June 2012 as a time limit for prescribing development and accession fees. I will only briefly refer to the compensation for the melioration drain and the irrigation fee that the income of the units of the district, or regional self-government, has not been called for justifiable reasons so far. These fees shall be paid to agricultural land of the units of the regional self-government in which the buildings for that purpose have been built, and shall be paid by the owners or other legal owners of the agricultural land. The reasons why these fees have not been paid, on the one hand are the degradation, the devastation of the system of detailed melioration disposal, and on the other hand because there were no more significant watering options... on systematic buildings for that purpose. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] Today, however, opportunities have changed significantly. Unfortunately, inadequate treatment of waste water is the second biggest problem of environmental protection in Croatia. This specifically means that we have an inadequate system or even a lack of wastewater management system. In Croatia, 60% of the population has no sewage connection, and sewerage is a basic prerequisite for the construction of wastewater treatment systems. Although research has been carried out in continental Croatia has proven the presence of a large amount of high-quality mineral water, some of the sources are thousands of years old and the export of water from Croatia is negligible, and when there is water it is exported as raw materials in tanks and tanks, and not as a final product, the brand by which Croatia should be known in the world. However, the state's irresponsibility towards water does not stop as a strategic resource, so in many cities in Croatia, the losses from the water network amount to a scandalous 40%. In addition, the population of Croatia is horrified about the importance of water protection as an extremely important resource, and this is seen from the daily conduct of the vast majority of the Croatian population. The population, despite the existence of taps that are easy to stop the flow of water when washing hands, teeth, dishes, etc., does not use that possibility. Many citizens and citizens today produce various waste oils that irreversibly pollute millions of liters of water. It is regrettable that the Government of the Republic of Croatia does not recognise the importance of education of the population on importance and responsible behaviour, and responsible water consumption as a priority political issue. Under HDZ, no public campaign has been implemented to draw the public's attention to this extremely significant problem. What we can expect because of this irresponsible behaviour of the government is a recent blow to the budgets of the population, i.e. a major increase in water prices. It is unfortunately a modus operand of this government, i.e. financial discipline of the population, not education and resource savings. In addition, should we mention daily examples of unscrupulous extraction of gravel from the troughs of our rivers that irretrievably pollute our underground waters, so - called underground waters. the strategic resource of the 21st century. The fact is that we are constantly witnessing the urban development of cities that are constantly increasing and now 50% of the Croatian population is living in them, and thus the need for building materials taken from nature is constantly increasing. A harmful effect on the environment that causes so-called gravel and other practices are almost always large scales. Unfortunately, it is not crucial that such practices are made more or less advantageous by unconscious individuals. It is the opening of early water layers in this way, a horizon allowing direct introduction of pollution into the underground, with the help of state structures that are lurking in both eyes of such illicit practices. Unless there is an actual political will to effectively inspect by stopping looking through the fingers of different concessionaries and those who illegally extract sand and gravel, unless there is a will to conduct thorough and uncompromising investigations, monetary and prison sanctioning crimes that are wrongly applied to the environment, local populations and the national economy, which is unfortunately evident from this Water Law proposal, I fear that we cannot look at the future with optimism. For all things previously said, unfortunately in the SDP, we need to conclude that today the state is treating the water as a mother, and this maternal relationship with the waters will continue in the future, and even worsen, according to the bill we are discussing today. Because of our awareness of the strategic dimension of water in the SDP Club, we are committed to passing a quality law that would regulate water protection and management. We also advocate the development of quality environmental impact studies that would truly protect this first-class Croatian public interest, not in order to make studies, and what the situation has today favoured the interest of investors, whether it is private or state capital. In the SDP, we also consider it necessary to control strictly the exploitation of groundwater and drinking water and to change the trend so far, i.e. to increase water exports as a final product, not as raw materials. In order to effectively protect this extremely important resource, it is necessary to increase the number of inspectors, inspection of water in strict application of laws and legal standards, including with the help of newly formed environmental police. The SDP also advocates the systematic education of Croatian citizens on the importance of water conservation and possible harmful effects and ways in which prevention can take place, for example to warn about the harmfulness of the spilling of motor oil into sewers. The law that lies before us is confusing, contradictory in many provisions, incomplete and inaccurate, lacking the necessary criteria that must contain quality laws. So the law instead of protecting water as a public good that belongs to all, as our most valuable resource, it wide opens the door to privatising water. In fact, the bill defines water as a common good, but the properties on which that water lies will now be able to be privately owned. This is unequivocally stated in Article 10 of this bill, which reads: "Regulating and protective structures are public goods in general use owned by the Republic of Croatia. Exceptionally, in paragraph 1 of this Article, the regulatory and protective structures of an investor who is a private person are owned by those persons." The end of the quote. This is contrary to the provisions of the Property Act and other real rights which under real estate implies land, and all that is on that land belongs to that land. So the fundamental principle of property unity means that whoever owns the land is also the owner of everything on that land. The ownership of the land can be exceptionally excluded from the property of the facility only temporarily and on the basis of the right to build. The separation of ownership of land from the ownership of the building is possible only exceptionally and temporarily, based on the right to build in a certain period of time. According to the proposed Water Law proposal, there is a separation of property of the facility, i.e. water buildings and land on which these buildings were built, i.e. buildings would no longer belong to the land, and the owners of the land would also be owners of water buildings. Or in other words, in practice, this will mean that the owner of the building of the accumulated lake is the owner of the lake’s land, then that he owns an artificial riverbed or melioracian canal of land on which the river is located, i.e. the canal and the like. Therefore, the separation of ownership of water structures from the ownership of land particles, and as defined by this proposal of the law, is a violation of the legal system of property unity and the Law on ownership and other real rights and the creation of complete legal confusion. This proposal for a law is contrary to the concession law because it contains a series of provisions which provide for the award of a concession for the use of a public water goods without a competition, i.e. by direct contract, for example on the basis of a water legal permit which, under its content, is the right of concessions under a decision or sublegal act to be prescribed by the Minister or by the transfer of concession to another person. To say, these provisions open doors wide to corruption. This bill is also very liberal to those who profit enormously from the extraction of mineral raw materials in the area of importance for the water regime, and even to those who do so illegally. Thus, in Article 61 of the Law, the bill states: "A legal, i.e. natural person who is in the area of importance for the water regime and sanitary protection zones draws out sand and gravel without a concession agreement, that is to say, extracts a stone without acts prescribed by special rules in mining and water law, it is necessary to compensate for the damage done according to the decision on the level of compensation for gravel and sand that is made in accordance with the law governing the financing of the water economy." This provision of the law will surely scare very much the unscrupulous criminals who make enormous profits on these unorganic uses. In other words, criminals dealing with illegal gravel continue to be free, as far as the state is concerned, and will not fall off their heads. In fact, if they're caught, they'll just have to pay for the gravel, sand and stone at the legal price. We also consider the article 59, which represents nothing but an excuse for over-exploitation of gravel and sand quasi due to the work of editing and maintenance of inland waterways, maintenance of port and port aquariums and so on. Croatian waters are already implementing such projects, and in this sense I will mention a project which Croatia is leading under the alleged alibi of flood defences, measures of technical and economic maintenance of the Drava and Mura troughs at 9 locations plan to extract a million metres of cubic gravel, which will devast extremely valuable habitats. Namely, the Ministry of Regional Development, Forestry and Water Economy, which is proposed by the proposer of this proposal in September this year, issued a solution on the measures of technical and economic maintenance of the river Drava and Mura, which Croatia's waters are required to implement within 90 days past Croatian laws and public views. These locations are of exceptional natural value, they are listed on the list of European important habitats for wetlands and certain types of mammals, fish, reptiles and plants. The planned work would significantly disrupt the entrenched migration routes of game, as well as the migration pathways of fish at the time of deforestation, but it does not care about the Government of the Republic of Croatia, which in this bill is in Articles 114 and 115, stipulates that the interest in protecting human life and health and their property is stronger than the interests of an ecologically significant area, i.e. preserving biodiversity and the protection of natural values, thereby giving incredible freedom to build new regulatory and protective structures, building new buildings for water melliorization and maintenance. We would also like to stress that the proposal for a Water Law gives unlimited powers to Croatian waters in deciding on the acquisition of real and other rights on public water good, and these rights can only belong to the Government of the Republic of Croatia. In this proposal, Croatia's water law is defined as an agency with just the most amazing beauty of the area of action. This bill establishes another agency, the Water Regulation Agency for which the bill does not show at all what it should do except to catch new HDZ personnel. Similarly, we are afraid to plan another new body of the Water Institute that should be engaged in scientific and professional and other similar activities. When we add the two agencies and the institute to both the RES and the National Water Council, I'm afraid we'll get total confusion and chaos where we won't know who's drinking and who's paying, which we'll be sure to have tragic consequences for water protection in Croatia. The weaknesses of this bill are also a whole series of provisions which are provided that the sublaw, the Government Regulation or the Rules of Procedure of the Minister will regulate issues which cannot, by their nature, be prescribed by a sublegal act, but must be governed by the law. One of the stupidities of this law is Article 30 of the Law, which determines that the minister will prescribe the content, procedure and methodology of the production of the Water Management Strategy. Which is why the minister must prescribe how the strategy will look really unclear to us, because it is known what structure must have any strategy and so does the water management strategy. In other words, a completely pointless red tape that is definitely not the place in the law. And at the very end of this presentation on behalf of the SDP club, I would like to say that I oppose this bill because it is unorganic, it does not recognise, it does not protect water as a strategic national resource because it is not in accordance with our legal system because this bill wide opens the door to privatising water, which is for us as parliamentary representatives and advocates who must protect public and not private interest absolutely unacceptable, and because this bill encourages corruption and unnecessary awakening of a politically similar administration. Unfortunately, we must conclude that in the last two weeks, by ruling with a visibly and completely eyeslessly, they only care about the protection of the material interests of lobbys close to the top of the government, not the protection of public interests, which should be their job. This is visible not only from the proposal of the Water Law, which we are discussing today, but also from the Agricultural Land Act and the Golf Courses Act. Since we have put all these privatisation laws that remind the law on conversion and privatisation from the 1990s into the parliamentary procedure, and the Law on Golf Courses even in an emergency procedure in the holidays when people, or the public, are not quite up to politics, it speaks sufficiently of the right intentions of the promoters and this is the Government of the Republic of Croatia.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23704	Thank you. On behalf of the HDZ club, distinguished representative Nevenka Marinovic. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23705	Dear Vice-President of the Croatian Parliament, the Honorable Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues. In front of us, the Water Act, which the Government of the Republic of Croatia has submitted to the Croatian Parliament in the time provided for by the National Plan for the harmonisation of the legislation of the Republic of Croatia with the acquis of the European Union. The water crisis felt by a large part of the world is not caused by too little water, but by an inadequate way of water management that results in billions of people suffering from water shortages. Some estimates suggest that in the near future, some 3 billion people will have less than a thousand hundred cubic cc's of renewable water resources per capita per year. That amount you'll agree to is insufficient. Croatia is fortunately listed in a group of water-rich countries where water and water problems have not yet been tightened and water resources are not a limiting factor for the time being. Moreover, the Republic of Croatia, by the availability and wealth of water sources, is 5th in Europe and 42nd in the world and has an extremely high potential. The balances of surface and underground waters show that the Republic of Croatia has large indivisible spatial and time-assigned quantities of surface and underground waters. Accordingly, water management bodies have powers, obligations and opportunities to design quality and harmonised solutions sustainable for all parts of the water system and all activities of the water system, depending on the water of the dependent economy. I think it is also an opportunity to remind myself that a water economy has been organised in our area since 1876 and the first Water Law Act was passed by the Croatian Parliament in 1891. The 1995 Water Act was the first step in adapting water management, political conditions after the country's independence and changes in 2005 began to bring the European Community's requirements closer to the declared water management policy in the community. The adoption of a water management strategy adopted by this Parliament in July of this year has paved the way for the full transformation of the water management system in the Republic of Croatia, meaning in accordance with the procedures of the European Union, but the same document also defined strategic provisions, namely that water is a natural wealth of interest to the Republic of Croatia, that the possibility of privatising water resources rights is excluded, that integrated water management and water division is managed, that decentralisation of water management is introduced, the extraction of infrastructure from municipal companies owned by local self-government units, awarded concessions for public water supply to units of local self-government, not operators, but also the reform of the municipal sector. With this seventh edition of the Water Act, the requirements of the legal property of the European Union and all those strategic provisions of the recently adopted strategy are fully translated into the legal system of the Republic of Croatia. So a total of 10 regulations have been transposed. It's mostly about directives. A number of new water management instruments have also been introduced in the country but also used valuable national experience gained in continuous, organised water management for more than 130 years. I would like to point out here that the new law has created a successful merger between traditional water management patterns and new management instruments to ensure primarily sustainable water management that reduces and overcomes conflict between human and environmental water needs, but also increases the level of protection of human health and the environment as a whole, as well as an increase in the degree of sustainability of eco-systems and the protection of biodiversity. Further meeting increased water requirements for certain qualitys in the requested quantities, but also reducing water pollution and removing water pollution to dangerous substances, as well as reducing uncertainty related to floods and droughts. The aim is to protect and quality water means to all water, surface, underground, transitional and coastal, with special care for the management of the quality of water for human use. The institutional water management structure rests on the principle of decentralisation with clear responsibilities in the national, regional and local water sectors. At the Club of the Croatian Democratic Communities, we believe that it is good for the national water sector to exercise its responsibilities in water management, in managing public water good, in developing and managing the system of basic melliorative draining and irrigation, and in implementing the national investment policy in the regional and local water sector. Yes to, and that the regional sector is fulfilling our responsibilities in the development and management of the system of detailed melliorative draining and irrigation and the local water sector, which means associated cities and municipalities to exercise competence in the development and management of public water supply systems, wastewater disposal and coastal waters, as well as waste water treatment. The reform of the municipal sector provides for the uniformity and improvement of the quality of services as well as more efficient and quality management of public water supply and draining and water treatment systems. In addition to the development of the water information system, the establishment of a water institute as well as the Agency for the Regulation of Water Services, the implementation of the above will be significantly facilitated. This law also carried out harmonisation with horizontal laws, meaning the Law on concessions, spatial arrangements, environmental protection, nature protection and the Law on navigation and ports. In the Club of the Croatian Democratic Community, we hold that we are making this law at the right time to avoid the problems that some countries already have, and this is possibly the creation of a water monopoly when water prices, when water prices are rising, water management quality declines, investments are reduced, water, water is less available to many. France has just begun the process of reprivatisation by returning the largest private company under public supervision. In conclusion, I'm going to single out one more time why are we going to support this law first reading? Because there is no privatisation means either direct or indirect water resources or privatisation of water-communal infrastructure. Water management is decentralised. Public water supply and disposal buildings, meaning public water supply and disposal buildings are public goods and in general use. The water-communication infrastructure cannot be the subject of enforcement, cannot be alienated or become part of the bankruptcy mass. We believe that this is how we protect this very valuable resource. Thank you.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23769	Mr. Vice President, honourable MPs and MPs. The next two items of the agenda are a proposal to amend the Penal Code and a proposal for a new Criminal Procedure Act. These proposals include the key content of the announced anti-mafia package in the jurisdiction of the Ministry of Justice. First things first, about changing the Penal Code. Our Penal Code has been completed several times already. We need a more conceptual new Penal Code. I will appoint a task force to make it by the end of the year, and I expect it to complete its work by autumn 2009. The amendments to the Penal Code that I am proposing today therefore include only the most hasty and simplest content. The amendments provided for in the proposal may be systematised in four groups. In the first group, there are amendments to which the Penal Code is brought into line with international law and both the Conventional Laws of the United Nations and of the Council of Europe and the acquis of the European Union. The second group includes those changes that strengthen criminal opposition to organised crime and corruption. The third group of amendments seeks to improve the protection of children and minors against sexual abuse. Finally, in the fourth group, there are several amendments that remove some indetermination of the existing legal text or are nomotechnically modified. The harmonisation of the Penal Code with international law is mainly about terrorism. In this respect, the most important amendment to Article 169 is that of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and of the The Penal Code in order to comply with the Framework Decision of the Council of the European Union on the fight against terrorism. Article 169 is amended as follows: The Penal Code shall be amended in the name and content to comply with the definition of terrorism referred to in Article 1 of the Framework Decision by removing the distinction between terrorism and international terrorism. At the same time, by eradicating the criminal part of anti-state terrorism Article 141 and the criminal part of the diversion of Article 143, the duality of terrorist incriminations in the light of the protection, anti-state and international terrorism which is in line with the requirements of the European Commission as well as trends in comparative law shall be abandoned. In addition, new acts of public encouragement to terrorism have been introduced, i.e. Article 169a and the recruitment and training of terrorism in Article 149b of the KZ in order to comply with the Council of Europe Convention on the Prevention of Terrorism. In order to comply with the International Convention on the Prevention of Part of Nuclear Terrorism, Article 172, the abuse of nuclear substances has been amended and extended to ban the abuse of nuclear and radioactive material, which is a broader concept. The amendments to the Penal Code in 2007 partially comply with the text of the Convention for the Protection of Financial Interests of the European Communities, but further corrections of the legal text need to be made in order to bring full harmonisation. The proposed amendment to the provisions of Article 15(2) shall also extend to all decisions which have been finalised by the parties to the procedure in another State. In this respect, it is also proposed to amend the transitional and final provisions of Article 190a in order to prescribe that the provision of Article 15(2) shall apply from the date of accession of the Republic of Croatia to the European Union. The most important changes concern strengthening the instruments against organised crime and corruption. One of the key measures to effectively combat organised crime and corruption is the confiscation of illegally acquired assets. New Article 82. The KZ represents only the heart of the anti-mafia package and therefore deserves a more detailed comment. This Regulation shall enter The KZ is based on the principle that no one can retain the property benefit achieved by a criminal offence. This benefit shall be taken away by a court ruling which determines that the offence has been committed, but if it is a work of the jurisdiction of the USKOK, therefore part of organised crime and corruption is assumed to have been acquired by the perpetrator’s total property by criminal act and shall be taken away unless the perpetrator makes it likely that its origin is lawful. In order to avoid the robbery of property being hidden by its transfer to other persons, it is predicted to be taken away even when it is legally based on the perpetrator’s spouse or extramarital partner, and other relatives. The wrongly acquired property benefit will also be taken away when fictitious legal transactions have been transferred to third natural or legal persons who have not acquired it in good faith. This amendment to the Penal Code creates favourable conditions for a successful operation to remove organised crime and corruption from Croatian society. Jumping departments are also responsible for punishing mobsters and for confiscating their assets starting their work by January 1st 2009. This booth still resonates with the cynic sayings that he's bailing on it. After adopting this amendment, it will no longer be true, but it will be worth who tries to bail again will be taken away from him all that he has ever pitted. In Article 175, trade in human beings and slavery has added a new qualified form of criminal offence, its initiation by the official person. Article 175(2) also criminalises child trafficking for illegal adoption. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The Penal Code has been amended in the name of money laundering modeled for parallel law and has been supplemented in content. According to the current legal solution, the object of the store is money, i.e. I quote: "The object or the right of the money provided to the person who knows it was obtained by a criminal act." Such a formulation excludes the possibility of prosecution if criminal acts are directly supplied to anything other than money, e.g. some object, right or some other property benefit. By amending paragraph 1, the facility also extends to items, rights or property benefits which the perpetrator knows was created or obtained by criminal acts. One of the ways organised crime strives to influence criminal justice is an inappropriate influence on witnesses and other persons who may cooperate with law enforcement authorities. Privileges and other forms of influence are most often and not directed against persons who, in a formal sense, already have witness status, but against those whom members of organised criminal groups assume that such status could be acquired during the procedure. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day The Penal Code and the so-called So-called Penal Code. Presumptive witnesses are protected from illegal pressures. Furthermore, the existing criminal offence of detecting the identity of the protected witness referred to in Article 305a of the Penal Code shall ensure criminal protection exclusively against the person already involved in the protection programme. The extension of this provision and to the person who is or will be examined as a protected witness in the criminal proceedings shall ensure enhanced criminal protection of those persons, as well as the social interest of more effective control of organised crime. I'm moving on to proposals on improving child and minor protection against sexual abuse. According to current solutions of the institution dealing with education, education, care and care of children and minors in the employment of new employees, they cannot obtain any insight into the criminal record of whether the candidate has previously been convicted of criminal offences in Chapter 14, so crimes against sexual freedom and disorder. According to this amendment, data from the criminal record shall be made known to the person who has been duly convicted of a criminal offence under Chapter 14 of the Criminal Code only provided that those offences are involved, that a reasoned and justified request has not yet been made and that rehabilitation has not yet taken place for those acts. The circle of authorising officers to access criminal records is extended to the solution so far, yet limited only to those entities registered to carry out the activities of education, care, care and education of children and minors. As regards other exceptions in the context of the possibility of using data from criminal records, data from the criminal record of the security measure imposed on the prohibition of the conduct of calls, activities or duties on the perpetrator of a criminal offence will be supplied exceptionally to the authorities responsible for keeping a register of persons carrying out the activities concerned. And finally, from the amendments which remove some indeterminates of the existing legal text, an important amendment to Article 308 of the play, a ban on security measures and the legal consequences of the conviction. In contrast to the solution under which the criminal responsibility has been made, only the person subject to the security measure has been able to implement these measures, this amendment is incriminating and avoiding those measures by the person concerned. The other changes are mostly nomotechnical, so I wouldn't particularly comment on them. Thank you.
