ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23805	Thank you. Honorable Vice President, esteemed MPs and MPs. In front of you, the Final Proposal for a Code on Criminal Procedure as one of the fundamental laws of the Croatian legal order and the terms of the constitutional principles applied in relation to the state authority according to fundamental rights and freedoms of citizens in the exercise of the function of legitimate public punishment. Unlike other laws, the proposal followed the principles of four years. On the proposal itself, work lasted two years. It should be pointed out that more than 30 experts participated from the drawing up of the principle through the first working group to the editorial group. Of the five professors at the time, three participated in these bodies, three Supreme Court judges, a municipal court judge, five deputy state attorneys, four judges of county courts, a representative of the Croatian Bar Association, six police officers, etc. The proposal rests on the idea of reforming the criminal proceedings in the direction of conversion from a mixed type of criminal procedure to a criminal procedure of the acoustic type. This transformation leads to a new concept of many procedural institutions. Following this as a key reform of the criminal proceedings, the reform of the previous procedure can be singled out, a consistent separation of the data collection function for the indictment carried out by the State's lawyer from the decision-making process which is the right and duty of the judge. A judicial investigation is hereby initiated, the introduction of a criminal investigator as a body acting at the request of a state attorney, the introduction of a judge as a new decision-making body in the previous proceedings, instead of the current cumulation of the functions of research and decision-making in the person of the investigating judge, the introduction of new forms of accelerated proceedings, the resolution of easier cases through the settlement of an agreement, the short-term prosecution with the appropriate compensation of the victim, the simplification of the conduct of preparations for the debate, the extension of the scope of the parties' agreement on punishment, the reform of the shortened procedure, the improvement of procedural rules governing measures for effective action such as measures to ensure the presence and measures of the procedural discipline, the reform of the rules on certain procedural units and certain procedural actions. I'll give you a brief overview of the most important news on the heads. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. It shall contain general provisions on the rights and duties of the State's These rights are regulated in accordance with the new procedural position of the State's attorney. The motion abolishs the court's investigation, no more an investigative judge, the state's attorney is the subject leading the investigation of the Dominus Litis. According to the new proposal, during the criminal proceedings, a neutral entity deciding on rights is establishing a balance between the opposing parties, the state lawyer and the defendant and, where necessary, regulating their relations. In the previous procedure, there is primarily the judge of the investigation and later the second body of the court. In the previous procedure, he decides but no longer investigates. In the previous procedure, the judge becomes a guarantee of the protection of rights and freedoms, the third in the conflict between the two sides. Chapter 5 is governed by the rights of the victim and the damages. For the first time in Croatian law, these provisions introduce a victim among participants of special rights proceedings. The victim has been defined in terms, taking into account the notional definition in international sources and foreign legislation. The victim has special rights because the criminal act violated her right, not because it caused damage. The proposal introduces quite certain duties of the body of criminal proceedings in dealing with the victim. It should be further mentioned that the legal position of the victim is also defined according to some important personal traits of a child, a minor, a victim of a crime against sexual morality. This sought to regulate the right to respect the victim's privacy as a person who had already experienced the trauma of committing a criminal offence and to prevent or at least reduce secondary victimisation by inappropriate treatment during the procedure. Chapter 9 shall be governed by measures to ensure the presence and other precautionary measures. Because of the human rights procedure, these provisions have special importance. In this part of the proposal, significant news has been introduced in relation to the current CAP, three of which are particularly important. Detention as the first and short-term measure to ensure the presence of a state lawyer who replaces detention towards the current CAP. An appeal to the state's attorney's decision is decided by the judge of the investigation. Another important news has to do with the conduct of the arrests. The proposal sets out that the arrester surrenders himself to a special authorised police officer, a detention officer responsible for the arrester and detainee. The officer is responsible for the legality of the arrest and the regularity of dealing with the arrestee and the detainee, so the person deprived of liberty at the time until she is not in the prison of investigation. The detention supervisor is not involved in the investigation of a criminal offence. The third news is an investigative prison, which may take longer time before a court ruling. It's a substance detention against the current CAP. Chapter 17 refers to the investigation. There are provisions on a new state-owned law enforcement investigation as part of a reform of criminal procedural legislation that is of paramount importance. The news is that an investigation can also be carried out against an unknown perpetrator. A familiar condition relative to the current CAP has been reduced. The main news of the proposal is that the investigation is conducted by a state attorney alone or entrusted by a warrant to conduct an investigation or police action. The suspect doesn't have a legal remedy against the warrant. The investigator is the new subject of Croatian criminal proceedings. It's a person who, under legal authority, takes actions under the orders of a state attorney. The judge of the investigation is also a new entity in the Croatian criminal proceedings. It shall decide on the whole of the complex and mutually conditional relations of the participants of the proceedings. The judge in particular decides on procedures in the freedom and rights of citizens. In addition, the judge of the investigation shall carry out an evidence hearing in the law of the intended cases. A special place, given the importance, has a provision that regulates the content of the lesson on rights. It is a novelty in the Croatian criminal procedure. This provision not only provides for certain content of the lesson on rights but also of the time in which it must be delivered to the defendant during the course of the proceedings. In Chapter 18, there are rules on evidence stores. An important innovation has been introduced in the search. An emergency search of the person and means of transport under specific prescribed conditions shall be carried out on the basis of a national legal order. The search warranted by the state's attorney is subject to a later court order. Chapter 24 contains provisions for exceptional legal medicinal products. The most important thing is the novelty of abandoning these medicinal products from the system of exceptional mitigation of penalties. The common rules of the shortened procedure are in Chapter 25, and Chapter 26, which regulates the procedures for issuing a criminal order and giving a court notice. The shortened procedure is based on the proposal for criminal offences for which a prison sentence is prescribed for up to 12 years. It's a general provision. By law, certain offences are excluded from the general provision and are subject to regular proceedings. The decision-making area of the state's attorney has been expanded according to the principle of opportunity. The number of criminal acts has been reduced. For offences for which a shortened investigation procedure is prescribed, in no case shall it be mandatory. The introduction of new forms of accelerated procedures in the regular and shortened procedure follows modern legislative tendencies in the most developed European countries. This is what I aim to achieve two key objectives. The first dispensation of courts from holding discussions in cases where the situation is such that they can be terminated without debate or with a debate only on the imposition of sanctions. In addition to appropriate application practice, significant acceleration of the process can be expected, reduction of the total number of hearing hearings and long-term very significant budgetary savings. The second objective is to concentrate the court on cases in which a debate is needed because of their weight or complexity. This, in addition to appropriate practice, should be important to raise the quality of the process and improve the dynamics of team work, as a rule to complex subjects. The expanded whole of accelerated procedures in the new CAP could result in a reduction of the number of complete debates for the circus 40%, with appropriate practice in the shortened procedure. This is very significant for the entire area of criminal trial. Transitional and final provisions have great importance given that many issues have to be regulated by specific laws and regulations. The proposal provides for a stepwise entry into force of the transitional and final provisions until the adoption of a specific law, the provisions of the Criminal Procedure Act on Real Jurisdiction, Articles 16 to 22 and Chapter 29 and 30 will apply. The first have to regulate the Law on the Organisation of Courts and the second Law on Rehabilitation and Compensation to Unfounded Arrested and Convicted Persons. The provisions of the Law on Courts governed by the Organisation and the jurisdiction of the courts will apply from the entry into force of the Criminal Procedure Act. In the Croatian Parliament, the following conclusion was reached after the debate on the Bill's Proposal in Criminal Procedure at a session held in July 2008: "The Proposed Code on Criminal Procedure is hereby adopted." All objections, proposals and opinions will be addressed to the applicant in order to prepare the final draft law. After first reading the text of the draft was submitted to all relevant bodies, the Supreme Court of the Republic of Croatia, the State Attorney's Office. The Academy of Legal Sciences also organised a thematic meeting on 30.9.2008, and the Ministry of Justice round table on 12.11.2008. Since June 2008, there have been only a few objections and proposals from the state authorities, above all, from the ministries that have been discussed, and some have been respected. Compared to the text of the draft law which was at the first reading in the Croatian Parliament, certain differences arised in the final proposal as a result of respecting the proposals and opinions from the debate in the Croatian Parliament of the Ministry of Interior, the State Attorney's Office of the Republic of Croatia, the participants of the thematic sessions of the Croatian Academy of Legal Sciences and the Round Table. In relation to the text of the Code on Criminal Procedure, which passed the first reading of most of the remarks introduced, the nomotechnical and technical nature and of the major substantive changes are as follows: Article 10 of the proposal introduces a new paragraph 4 which provides that the judicial decision cannot be based solely on the evidence provided by the infringement of the Constitution, the Constitution, the law or international law of the guaranteed rights of defence, the rights of dignity, the reputation and honor and the right of non-violability of personal and family life in the procedure for serious forms of criminal offences for which the infringement of the rights in respect of strength and nature is significantly less than the severity of the offence. Furthermore, Article 216 of which refers to the conduct of the investigation by considering the occurrences of criminal offences in practice accepted the suggestion that the mandatory conduct of the investigation is limited only to the most serious offences and that in other cases it depends on the judgment of a State lawyer. The indictment is always subject to judicial investigation and the defendant has the right and duty of a possible indictment which is not sufficiently justified to return to the supplement, and in the law provided for by the Court of Justice may dismiss those charges. In Article 248, which regulates the conduct of the search of the apartment and of the person who has entered the obligation to submit a copy of the records on the search of the apartment or person to whom the search is carried out. In Article 265, the first sentence of banking secrecy is left out because the bank secret is governed by other regulations. The law prescribes a way of obtaining information which is a secret bank by installing the issue in certain cases by a special law on USKOK. The provisions of Article 327 have been amended in a way that is now subject to the obligation of the body conducting the criminal procedure to take samples of biological material for a criminal offence for which a sentence of at least 6 months is prescribed. It is also prescribed in paragraph 6 that data collected by molecular-genetic analysis shall be kept as a rule for 20 years. An important amendment to Article 501 which regulates the renewal of proceedings and where it is explicitly prescribed that criminal proceedings may be renewed regardless of the presence of convicts. We also state the differences that have arisen and related to transitional and final provisions. The proposal provides for certain provisions to enter into force on 1 January 2009. These are provisions relating to the secrecy of the investigation and the renewal of the proceedings. Application of the law in the cases referred to in Article 21 On 1 July 2009, and the final application of the law in all criminal cases since 1 September 2011, the USKOK Act provides that the whole of 1.1.2009 with these exceptions is to enter into force. This would briefly change anything in relation to the proposal and the final proposal for the text. Thank you very much.

This law must ensure and guarantee a balance between the effective protection of society against the most dangerous attacks, but a way to ensure that perpetrators of criminal offences are found, processed and based on the law punished, while at the same time guaranteeing that the principles of human rights and fundamental freedoms in the conduct of criminal proceedings are respected. This is a law also expected in the area of criminal justice to meet the negotiating criteria in the accession process of the Republic of Croatia to the European Union, which means speeding up criminal proceedings and reducing the number of outstanding old cases. Of course, the legislative framework itself will not be enough, there is still a series of measures being implemented through the justice reform strategy, but certainly this is one of the essential tools. This proposal for a new criminal procedural law is headed towards reforming the criminal procedure, i.e. in the transformation from a mixed type of criminal procedure to a criminal procedure of the acoustic type. This transformation leads to a series of conceptual changes in many procedural institutes. We are aware that the future abandonment, i.e. the absence of more inquisitor maximum, will be the most difficult to accept to judges because the long-term tradition and concept of the criminal procedure of Croatia is different. ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23875	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament. So in short, what are the reasons for this law being passed. By adopting the Capital Market Act, which enters into force on 1 January 2009, in a comprehensive and systematic manner, a regulated area of the capital market including the regulation of investment services, companies and other institutions of the capital market, as well as regulated and effective supervision of the entire system. As a comprehensive reform of criminal legislation is foreseen for the first half of the following year, and the Capital Market Act enters into force on 1 January, with the effect of that Capital Market Act, it is necessary to introduce and regulate offences against the capital market, which is achieved by this proposal. The bill regulates criminal acts against capital markets and so on. Under one, a criminal offence of use, disclosure and recommendation of privileged information. Two, criminal act of market manipulation. Under three, a criminal offence is an unauthorized provision of investment services. And under four, the criminal offence of the unauthorized conduct of the work of a tied representative. More serious forms of crime shall be considered if these offences against the capital market are supplied with a significant material benefit or cause significant material damage, i.e. by means of abuse of the use of privileged information, inter alia, if such privileged information is used by a person who is at the same time a member of the board of directors or supervisory board, i.e. he owns shares or shares in a company which is the issuer of a financial instrument, for whom the person makes use of the privileged information. What this bill seeks to achieve, which is to introduce an effective instrument for combating the exploitation of privileged so-called "priorities". Insider information and various forms of manipulation of the capital market, and we introduce an effective instrument to prevent unauthorized provision of investment services and to carry out business of a tied representative in such a way as to prescribe fines from fines to prisons of up to 5 years for serious forms of individual criminal offences. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2008-12-11-0.u23877	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President, the Honorable Representatives of the Ministry of Finance, the Honorable Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues. Very short. The Croatian Democratic Community's representative club supports the proposal for a law on criminal acts against the capital market. I think it's actually important to point out two things. So the Capital Market Act itself was adopted and will enter into force on 1 January 2009. This law establishes, among other things, an area or a regulated area and the business of persons authorised to carry out financial instruments transactions on the one hand and, on the other hand, to stress that I am the law, hence the Capital Market Act introduces European standards and with regard to market abuse. The introduction of these standards and one issue has therefore been raised, the issue of criminal liability for the unlawful conduct of certain specific offences which are present in this particular sphere, therefore business, and therefore it was precisely necessary to prescribe criminal offences and sanctions for the initiation of that type, specific types of criminal offences, in order to fully regulate the capital market in all their segments and those participants in the processes that occur within the capital market, so when illegal actions occur, when illegal actions are committed or offences themselves. These crimes are by this brief law, as already stated in the opening statement. They are not only mentioned, prescribed, certain sanctions. These are specific crimes and are therefore found in this special law, although there have also been some opinions that, since it is a catalogue of criminal offences, they should also be in criminal material law. But here is a solution to support it, because this solution corresponds to the specific needs, so the time in which we go to sanction in the best possible way all those illegal behaviours of those participants in the capital market itself. Therefore, when a criminal offence is carried out which is prescribed by this law, and Article 2 of this Law requires that the provisions of the Penal Code apply to the offences prescribed by this law. It is important to note in particular what criminal acts are involved. So these are primarily the use, detection and recommendation of privileged information. Then. So it's about privileged information. Then manipulation of the market. Furthermore, the unauthorized provision of investment services and the unauthorised performance of the business of a tied representative. That we do not engage in an analysis of each of these criminal offences from the very title of a criminal offence, we see the types of unlawful conduct being committed and it is indeed necessary to incriminate such a type of unlawful conduct, i.e. to prescribe a special criminal offence with a sanction so that the perpetrators of these offences can be properly punished.
